## Rate curve

Story 2.5 writes the one rate-curve law, for the binaural beat rate. Story 2.6 applies it unchanged, with the same integral, the same cycle cosine, and the same cycles-to-units rule, to the theta rate and the gamma rate; no new formula enters this section. v0.1 has only this sinusoidal cycle: there are no piecewise-linear curves, and no carrier takes a rate curve.

**Law.**

```
rate(t) = r + s * sin(2 * pi * t / P + phi0)
```

`r` is the center in Hz, `s >= 0` the swing in Hz, `P` the period in seconds, and `phi0` the start phase. A start phase of 0 degrees is at the center, rising; 90 degrees at the maximum `r + s`; 180 degrees at the center, falling; 270 degrees at the minimum `r - s`. The rate spans `[r - s, r + s]`.

**Recipe fields.** For the binaural beat rate: `r` is `binaural.beat_rate_hz`, and `binaural.beat_rate_cycle` holds the rest:

- `swing_hz`: `s`, at least 0 (no range row: the worst-case checks below bound it)
- `period_s`: `P` (range row `rate-cycle-period`, exactly 60 s)
- `start_phase_deg`: `phi0` in degrees (range row `rate-cycle-start-phase`, 0 to 360 inclusive; 360 is the same point as 0)

For the theta rate (Story 2.6): `r` is `theta_reference.rate_hz`, and `theta_reference.rate_cycle` holds the rest. For the gamma rate: `r` is `gamma_pac.rate_hz`, and `gamma_pac.rate_cycle` holds the rest. Each `rate_cycle` has the same three members, all three required inside it, under the same rows: `swing_hz` (no row), `period_s` (row `rate-cycle-period`), and `start_phase_deg` (row `rate-cycle-start-phase`).

`period_s` is locked to 60 s, the only period with a source (Lee et al. 2024, design log 2026-10-27 (n)), until a study supports others. The theta and gamma cycles use the same row, so the same 60 s, until a study supports others (Audio, 2026-11-14).

**Range checks, worst case.** With a cycle, right after the binaural checks of Binaural layer, Compile order, and in this order (first failure wins):

1. `swing_hz >= 0`: parameter `/binaural/beat_rate_cycle/swing_hz`, value the swing, rule `range.min`, limit `>= 0 Hz`.
2. The lowest rate `r - s` (binary64, one rounding) against row `beat-rate`: parameter `/binaural/beat_rate_cycle/swing_hz`, value `r - s`, for example limit `>= 0 Hz (row beat-rate)`.
3. The highest rate `r + s` (binary64, one rounding) against row `beat-rate`: same parameter, value `r + s`, for example limit `< 30 Hz (row beat-rate)`.
4. The highest right carrier `f_C + s` (binary64, one rounding, with `f_C` from Binaural layer, Oscillators) against row `binaural-carrier-right`: same parameter, value `f_C + s`, limit `< 1030 Hz (row binaural-carrier-right)`.
5. `period_s` against row `rate-cycle-period`: parameter `/binaural/beat_rate_cycle/period_s`, rule `range.min` or `range.max` (59.99 and 60.01 are both rejected).
6. `start_phase_deg` against row `rate-cycle-start-phase`: parameter `/binaural/beat_rate_cycle/start_phase_deg`.

**Range checks, theta and gamma (Story 2.6).** With a `rate_cycle`, right after the range checks of that layer's own fields (the theta reference's `carrier_hz`, `rate_hz`, and `am_depth_percent`; the gamma PAC layer's `carrier_hz`, `rate_hz`, `am_depth_percent`, and `coupling_phase_deg`), and in this order (first failure wins), with `<layer>` `theta_reference` or `gamma_pac` and `<row>` `theta-rate` or `gamma-rate`:

1. `swing_hz >= 0`: parameter `/<layer>/rate_cycle/swing_hz`, value the swing, rule `range.min`, limit `>= 0 Hz`.
2. The lowest rate `r - s` (binary64, one rounding) against row `<row>`: parameter `/<layer>/rate_cycle/swing_hz`, value `r - s`, for example limit `>= 1 Hz (row theta-rate)`.
3. The highest rate `r + s` (binary64, one rounding) against row `<row>`: same parameter, value `r + s`, for example limit `<= 60 Hz (row gamma-rate)`.
4. `period_s` against row `rate-cycle-period`: parameter `/<layer>/rate_cycle/period_s`.
5. `start_phase_deg` against row `rate-cycle-start-phase`: parameter `/<layer>/rate_cycle/start_phase_deg`.

The layers are checked in field order: theta reference, gamma PAC layer, binaural layer. The carrier rule (row `carrier-rule`; Theta reference, Recipe fields; Gamma PAC layer, Compile order) then judges each curved layer's carrier against its highest rate `r + s`, the worst case: for example a 200 Hz gamma carrier with a 45 +- 10 Hz cycle is rejected with parameter `/gamma_pac/carrier_hz`, value `200`, rule `carrier_rule`, limit `>= 220 Hz (4 x 55 Hz, row carrier-rule)`, though its center rate passes.

A cycle field that is not a number, a missing one, or an unknown one is a schema fault (rules `type`, `required`, `unknown_property`).

**Sleep cycle.** `r = 1.5`, `s = 1.5`, `P = 60`, `phi0 = 270` degrees: the beat rate cycles over 0 to 3 Hz every 60 s (Lee et al. 2024, design log 2026-10-27 (n)) and starts at 0 Hz, rising, so a session opens with no beat and the beat eases in. The sinusoidal shape of the law and the start phase are engineering defaults: Lee et al. 2024 does not state the waveform of the change (design log 2026-10-27 (n)), and no study sets the start phase (chosen by Audio on 2026-11-11). The Sleep starter recipe itself is not part of this story.

**Phase integral.** The beat phase is the integral of the rate, in cycles:

```
ideal:  Phi(t) = r * t + K * (cos(phi0) - cos(2 * pi * t / P + phi0)),    K = s * P / (2 * pi)
```

so `Phi(0) = 0` and `dPhi/dt = rate(t)`. The right ear phase is `f_L * t + Phi(t) = (f_L + r) * t + U(t)`, with `U(t) = K * (cos(phi0) - cos(2 * pi * t / P + phi0))`: the center term is an ordinary accumulator and `U` is one closed-form value per sample. No phase is summed from per-sample increments of a changing rate.

**Engine.** Once per plan:

```
P0    = coupling phase rule (start_phase_deg)                (Coupling, Coupling phase rule; 360 gives 0)
q_P   = 1.0 / period_s                                        binary64, one rounding
inc_P = phase increment rule (q_P, fs)                        cycle phase increment
K     = (swing_hz * period_s) * 0x1.45f306dc9c883p-3         two roundings; the constant is the binary64 nearest 1 / (2 pi)
c0    = cycle_cosine(P0)                                      the cosine of the start phase (Cycle cosine)
```

Per sample, in this order:

```
p_P[n]     = P0 + n * inc_P mod 2^64       cycle phase (P0 at n = 0)
c[n]       = cycle_cosine(p_P[n])          the cosine of the cycle phase (Cycle cosine)
v[n]       = K * (c0 - c[n])               two binary64 operations: c0 - c, then K times it
U[n]       = cycles-to-units rule (v[n])
phase_R[n] = phase_C[n] + U[n] mod 2^64    (Binaural layer, Oscillators)
```

At n = 0, `c[0]` is `c0` bit for bit (one function of the same phase), so `v[0] = K * +0.0 = +0.0`, `U[0] = 0`, and the right ear starts at phase 0 like the left. Every value is a pure function of `n`: two integer accumulators and one `cycle_cosine` (Cycle cosine).

**Engine, theta and gamma (Story 2.6).** Each curved layer has its own cycle: the once-per-plan lines above give its own `P0`, `q_P`, `inc_P`, `K`, and `c0` from its own `rate_cycle`, and the per-sample lines its own cycle phase `p_P[n]`, `c[n]`, `v[n]`, and `U[n]`. In place of the right-ear line:

```
phase_E[n] = n * inc_E + U_E[n] mod 2^64   theta envelope phase (Theta reference, Oscillators), U_E of the theta cycle
phase_M[n] = n * inc_M + U_M[n] mod 2^64   gamma envelope phase (Gamma PAC layer, Oscillators), U_M of the gamma cycle
```

`U_E[0] = U_M[0] = 0`, so sample 0 stays each envelope's 0 degree point. The carriers `phase_T` and `phase_G` take no `U`. The theta envelope, the smooth swell (Coupling), and the burst gate (Bursts) all read the curved `phase_E`, so coupling follows the theta curve with no rule of its own; AD-5 holds, since the gamma layer still reads only the theta envelope phase and plan constants. `W` (Theta reference, Fixed constants), `Hb`, and `Wb` (Bursts, Fixed constants) stay set at the center `rate_hz`. Without a cycle, `U = 0` for every sample and the layer is bit for bit what it was before Story 2.6.

**Cycle cosine.** `U` multiplies the cosine's error by `K`, up to about 143, so the cycle uses its own cosine, more exact than the engine sine (Sine, Engine sine), for both `c0` and `c[n]`. For a phase `p`:

1. **Quarter turn and fold, exact integer steps.** `q = p + 2^62 mod 2^64` (the cosine of `p` is the sine of `q`), then the engine sine's step 1 on `q`: read it as a signed 64-bit integer `s`, and fold into `[-2^62, 2^62]` with `s = 2^63 - s` above 2^62 and `s = -2^63 - s` below -2^62.
2. **Convert once.** `x = double(s) * 0x1.921fb54442d18p-62`, the engine sine's step 2.
3. **Odd Taylor polynomial to x^15, fixed Horner order.** With the coefficients of Vectors: cycle cosine coefficients (`Dk` the binary64 nearest to `+-1/k!`, sign alternating, `+` for k = 1; `D1` to `D11` equal the engine sine's `C1` to `C11`):

   ```
   z = x * x
   r = D15
   r = r * z + D13
   r = r * z + D11
   r = r * z + D9
   r = r * z + D7
   r = r * z + D5
   r = r * z + D3
   r = r * z + D1
   y = r * x
   ```

   Each `*` and `+` is one binary64 operation (no fused multiply-add).

**Error bound.** `|cycle_cosine(p) - cos(2 * pi * p / 2^64)| <= e_c = 1e-10` (row `cycle-cosine-error`, -200 dBFS), tested at the phases of Sine, Test resolution. The truncation error of the polynomial on `[-pi/2, pi/2]` is at most `(pi/2)^17 / 17!`, about 6.1e-12 (-224 dBFS), and the roundings add about 1e-16, so the bound holds with about 24 dB to spare. At phases 2^62 and 3 * 2^62 the fold gives `s = 0` and `y = +0.0` exactly, so `c0 = +0.0` at start phases 90 and 270 degrees. The engine sine is unchanged; only the rate cycles (the beat rate, and from Story 2.6 the theta and gamma rates) use the cycle cosine.

**Cycles-to-units rule.** A value `v` in cycles (binary64) becomes phase units, `(v mod 1) * 2^64` rounded:

```
if not (|v| < 2^52):  U = 0         NaN, both infinities, and |v| >= 2^52 (an integer, so 0 mod 1)
w = trunc(v)                        the integer part, toward zero; |v| < 2^52, so it fits a signed 64-bit integer
f = v - w                           binary64, exact: the fraction bits of v, in (-1, 1), with the sign of v
x = f * 0x1p64                      binary64, exact (a power-of-two scale)
U = x rounded to the nearest integer, ties to even, modulo 2^64
```

`f` is exact because it keeps only bits `v` already has. `|x| <= (1 - 2^-53) * 2^64 < 2^64`, so the rounded magnitude fits an unsigned 64-bit integer and no conversion can overflow. Rounding ties to even is symmetric, so a negative `x` gives `U = 2^64 - round(-x)` (and 0 where `round(-x)` is 0): `U` is the same point of the cycle as the exact `v mod 1`, within half a unit. Ties can happen only for `|x| < 2^52` (Vectors: cycles to units).

**Reference, theta and gamma (Story 2.6).** The theta segments and the burst segments are chosen by exact integer comparisons, so the Python reference builds an integer envelope phase from the ideal integral. Per curved layer it computes `P0` by the coupling phase rule, `inc_P` by the increment rule on `1.0 / period_s`, and `K = swing_hz * period_s / (2 * pi)` in binary64 (Python's `math.pi`), as for the beat rate (Binaural layer, Reference); the ideal `U[n] = K * (c0 - c[n])` in cycles, with the NumPy cosines of the signed phases `P0` and `p_P[n]` (in [-2^63, 2^63)) times `2 * pi / 2^64`; converts it to phase units by the cycles-to-units rule (`numpy.trunc`, then `numpy.rint` of the fraction times 2^64, negative values wrapped modulo 2^64); and adds it to `n * inc mod 2^64`. `reference/rate_curve.py` holds these steps for all three cycles, and `reference/binaural.py` takes its cycle constants and ideal `U` from it with unchanged output.

**Error bound, theta and gamma (Story 2.6).** As for the right ear (Binaural layer, Error bound), the engine envelope phase is within `delta = K * (eps_0 + e_c) + 2^-49 * K + 2^-64` cycles of the ideal integral; the reference's own binary64 `U` and its rounding to a phase unit add under `2^-52 * K + 2^-64`. An envelope whose largest slope is `L` per cycle therefore moves by at most `L * delta`. Every envelope is continuous across its segment boundaries (the sawtooth's drop edge starts at 1, where its linear rise ends), so where engine and reference phases straddle a boundary the same bound holds. Over every compile-accepted cycle at the 60 s period:

- **Theta:** `s <= 2.5` Hz (`r - s >= 1` and `r + s <= 6`), so `K` is below 23.9 and `delta` below 4.8e-9 cycles. On the sine shape (`L = pi`) the envelope moves under 1.5e-8. On a raised-cosine edge `L = (pi / 2) / (E * r)`, with `r` the center rate, so `L * delta` is at most `(s / r) * 1500 * (eps_0 + e_c)` plus under 1e-12, below 3e-7 since `s < r`; on a linear segment `L = 1 / (1 - E * r)`, at most about 1.06.
- **Gamma:** `s <= 15` Hz, so `K` is below 143.3 and `delta` below 2.9e-8; the gamma envelope (`L = pi`) moves under 9.1e-8.
- **Coupled factor on a curved theta:** the swell (`L = pi`) moves under 1.5e-8; the burst gate's edge has `L = (pi / 2) / (0.01 * r)`, so under 3e-7 as for the theta edge.

So each curved phase a tap reads adds at most 3e-7 (about -130 dBFS) to that tap's bound in its section: the theta tap within `1.5 * b + 2^-47 + 3e-7`, about -114.9 dBFS; the gamma tap, with a curved gamma envelope and a coupled factor on a curved theta, within `2 * b + 2^-46 + 6e-7`, about -111.7 dBFS; the stereo bus within `3.5 * b + 2^-45 + 9e-7`, about -107.1 dBFS; an output that adds a binaural ear within `4.5 * b + 2^-44 + 9e-7` plus that ear's cycle term (Binaural layer, Error bound), about -105 dBFS. Every bound is under row `reference-match` (-100 dBFS). On the Story 2.6 fixtures the vectors below read at most 2.4e-8 (about -152 dBFS).

**Frequency.** The instantaneous rate the right ear plays is `f_C + dU/dt`. The accumulators play `f_C` and `q_P` within the increment rule's frequency error (Core, Frequency error), so the center rate is off by under 1e-13 Hz and the cycle period by under 1e-11 s; the cycle cosine's error in `U` (Binaural layer, Error bound) changes only as slowly as the cycle phase. So the instantaneous rate follows the schedule within row `frequency-accuracy`, and the phase is continuous: from one sample to the next `U` changes by at most `s / fs` cycles plus the rounding, with no step. The frequency tool's phase mode (`tests/assertions/frequency.py`) measures both from the right ear tap. The same holds for a curved theta or gamma envelope, which plays `inc_E` or `inc_M` plus `dU/dt`; the frequency tool's envelope phase mode (Story 2.6) measures it from the mean-removed analytic envelope of a sine-shape theta tap or an uncoupled gamma tap.

**Seek.** Seek to frame `k` sets the cycle phase to `P0 + k * inc_P mod 2^64` and the center phase to `k * inc_C mod 2^64`; `U` needs nothing else, so seek is one step for any `k` and the block size cannot change a sample. A theta or gamma cycle phase is set the same way, with that cycle's own `P0` and `inc_P`, beside its envelope accumulator `k * inc_E` or `k * inc_M mod 2^64`.

### Vectors: theta and gamma rate cycles

Per Story 2.6 fixture, curved layer, and sample rate: the cycle's center (`rate_hz`), swing, period, and start phase, the lowest and highest rates `r - s` and `r + s` (one binary64 rounding each; Range checks, theta and gamma), the layer's envelope increment `inc_E` or `inc_M`, the cycle constants `P0`, `inc_P`, and `K` and the engine `c0` (Engine), and the session length `N` in frames. `theta-cycle-sine.json` and `theta-cycle-smoothed-on-off.json` are a 450 Hz carrier amplitude-modulated by the sine or smoothed on-off envelope at a theta rate that cycles over 1 to 6 Hz every 60 s (3.5 +- 2.5 Hz from the center, rising), 75 percent depth. `gamma-cycle-uncoupled.json` is a 250 Hz sine carrier amplitude-modulated at a gamma rate that cycles over 30 to 60 Hz every 60 s (45 +- 15 Hz), 100 percent depth, uncoupled. `swell-theta-cycle.json` and `bursts-theta-cycle.json` are a 450 Hz carrier amplitude-modulated by the sine envelope at a theta rate of 4 +- 2 Hz (75 percent depth) and a 250 Hz sine carrier amplitude-modulated at 40 Hz with a smooth swell or 50 ms bursts at 0 degrees, 100 percent depth. `gamma-cycle-swell-theta-cycle.json` is the swell fixture with the gamma rate also cycling, 45 +- 15 Hz. All are 120 s, with no binaural layer. `gamma-cycle-swell-theta-cycle.json` and `bursts-theta-cycle.json`, in this order, are the sources of the hash probe's `rate-curves-<fs>` components (5 s from frame 0, then 5 s after a seek to 55 s, each).

### Vectors: theta and gamma rate curve samples

Per Story 2.6 fixture and sample rate at sample `n`: each curved layer's cycle phase `p_P[n]` (`none` without a cycle), its offset `U` in phase units (0 without a cycle), and the curved envelope phases `phase_E` and `phase_M` (`n * inc + U mod 2^64`; the gamma columns are 0 without a gamma layer), the engine swell or burst gate (`none` uncoupled or past the end) and the theta tap, gamma tap, and stereo bus (also the output, since there is no binaural layer) as binary64 bits, with the ideal taps and bus at 25 digits: the ideal `U` (exact `K`, ideal cosines of the integer cycle phases) rounded to the nearest phase unit, the ideal envelope, swell, or gate of that integer phase (the same segments), and the ideal carrier sines. Indices cover the start, both sides of the first two block boundaries, sample 1000, both sides of each curved cycle's quarter points and 37 samples after each (off the whole second, where the 450 Hz carrier is not near 0), both sides of the seek point at 55 s, the last sample, and two samples past the end (case `past_end`: taps and output `+0.0`; the phase columns show the formulas only for reference).

## Loop

Story 2.7 writes looping sessions (Focus and Learn). A looping recipe has an opening that plays once, then a body that repeats until the session ends at the mode's max run time. Looping repeats the parameter schedule, never stored samples: every oscillator phase keeps running on the one engine clock (Core), so a seam is only a frame number, and no theta, gamma, carrier, or rate cycle phase can jump there. This section adds no oscillator formula. In this story the opening sounds like the body; the gain ramps of Story 4.4 will live in it, so the body stays steady.

**Recipe fields.** A top-level optional `loop`, an object with four members, all required inside it:

- `mode`: `focus` or `learn` (schema enum). It names the cap row: `focus` reads range row `max-run-time-focus` (45 min), `learn` reads `max-run-time-learn` (20 min) (Audio, 2026-11-14).
- `opening_s`: the opening length in seconds, at least 0.
- `body_s`: the body length in seconds, above 0.
- `max_run_time_min`: the max run time in minutes, against the mode's row.

A field that is not of its type, a missing one, an unknown one, or a `mode` outside the enum is a schema fault (rules `type`, `required`, `unknown_property`, `enum`); so a looping recipe without a cap is rejected by the schema. Every recipe without `loop`, which is every recipe written before Story 2.7, keeps its bytes and recipe hash and renders the same bits.

**Compile order.** The loop rules run after every range check of the earlier sections (the binaural checks last, with the rate cycles' worst cases of Rate curve, Range checks) and before the rules that follow the ranges (Theta reference, Gamma PAC layer, Coupling, Binaural layer, Compile order). In this order, first failure wins:

1. `max_run_time_min` against the mode's row: parameter `/loop/max_run_time_min`, rule `range.min` or `range.max`, limit for example `<= 45 min (row max-run-time-focus)`. Both rows are single values (min equal to max), so `focus` with 46 and `learn` with 45 are both rejected.
2. The session ends at the cap: `length_s` must equal `max_run_time_min * 60` (binary64, one rounding; exact for every value rule 1 accepts). Parameter `/length_s`, value `length_s`, rule `loop session ends at the max run time`, limit for example `= 1200 s (20 min x 60)`. A listener timer comes in Phase 3; until then the cap is the session length.
3. `opening_s >= 0`: parameter `/loop/opening_s`, rule `range.min`, limit `>= 0 s`.
4. `body_s > 0`: parameter `/loop/body_s`, rule `range.min`, limit `> 0 s`.
5. Whole samples, `opening_s` then `body_s` (Whole-sample rule): parameter the field, value the field, rule `loop time not whole samples`, limit `a multiple of 0.25 s (whole samples at 44100 and 48000 Hz)`.
6. Whole rate cycles: with any rate cycle (`theta_reference.rate_cycle`, `gamma_pac.rate_cycle`, `binaural.beat_rate_cycle`, in that order), `body_s` is a whole multiple of the cycle's `period_s` (Cycle rule): parameter `/loop/body_s`, value `body_s`, rule `loop body not whole rate cycles`, limit naming the period and its path, for example `a whole multiple of 60 s (/theta_reference/rate_cycle/period_s)`. Without a rate cycle there is no such rule.
7. `opening_s + body_s <= length_s` (binary64, one rounding; exact here): parameter `/loop/body_s`, value the sum, rule `loop opening and body exceed the session`, limit for example `<= 1200 s (length_s)`. So at least one whole body pass plays.

Values and limits are written as the shortest round-trip text of a binary64, as every range error is. Every rule runs in both compile modes, before `final.required`.

**Whole-sample rule.** A time `t_s` is a whole number of samples at both native rates when `t_s * 44100` and `t_s * 48000` are both integers, exactly (rational arithmetic, not the rounded product). A binary64 `t_s >= 0` is a dyadic rational `a / 2^k` with `a` odd or `k <= 0`; `t_s * 44100` is an integer only when `2^k` divides `44100 = 2^2 * 11025`, so `k <= 2`, and `t_s * 48000` only when `k <= 7` (`48000 = 2^7 * 375`). Both hold exactly when `k <= 2`, that is, when `4 * t_s` is an integer. The engine tests that, with no rounding:

```
x = t_s * 4.0      binary64, exact (a power-of-two scale)
whole when x >= 2^53, or when x equals its truncation toward zero
```

Below 2^53, `x` converts to an unsigned 64-bit integer and back exactly; every binary64 at or above 2^53 is an integer, and rule 7 then bounds both fields by the session length. The rounded product is not enough: the binary64 nearest 1/3, times 44100, rounds to exactly 14700, yet its exact product is not an integer (Vectors: whole samples). For a whole-sample `t_s` below 2^51 s, `t_s * fs` is an integer below 2^53, so the seconds-to-samples rule (Core) returns it exactly: `seconds_to_samples(t_s, fs) = t_s * fs`.

**Cycle rule.** `body_s` is a whole multiple of `period_s` when `body_s / period_s` is an integer, exactly. The engine tests:

```
q = body_s / period_s      binary64, one rounding
whole when q is an integer (the Whole-sample rule's integer test) and q * period_s == body_s (binary64, one rounding)
```

When the exact quotient is an integer below 2^53, the division and the product are exact, so the test passes. When it is not, the exact quotient is at least `1 / (4 * period_s)` from every integer (a whole-sample `body_s` is a multiple of 0.25 s, and the 60 s period of row `rate-cycle-period` is too), far more than the division's rounding for any body up to the 8 h session length, so `q` is not an integer and the test fails. So for every value that reaches it the test is the exact one. With a body of whole cycles, the ideal rate curve (Rate curve, Law) at time `opening_s + k * body_s + tau` equals the rate at `opening_s + tau` for every pass `k`: each pass carries the same schedule.

**Frames.** Once per plan, with the seconds-to-samples rule (Core), every value an exact integer:

```
O = seconds_to_samples(opening_s, fs)    opening end: the join, where the first body pass starts (0 when opening_s is 0)
B = seconds_to_samples(body_s, fs)       body length in frames
N = seconds_to_samples(length_s, fs)     session end: the frame count (Stub chain)
seam k = O + k * B                       for k = 1, 2, ... while O + k * B < N
M = (N - O - 1) div B                    the seam count, integer division; O + B <= N by rule 7
```

The opening covers frames `[0, O)`; body pass `k` (k = 0 to M) covers `[O + k * B, min(O + (k + 1) * B, N))`. When `N - O` is not a whole number of bodies, the last pass is cut at the cap. A seam at `N` would be the end, so it is not a seam. The frames are the same on every platform.

**Per sample.** Nothing changes. Every tap and the output at frame `n` are the formulas of the earlier sections at `n` (Stub chain, Theta reference, Gamma PAC layer, Coupling, Bursts, Binaural layer, Rate curve): every carrier, envelope, and cycle phase is its start plus `n * inc mod 2^64` (plus the cycle offset `U[n]` where a rate cycle applies), with no reset at the join or at a seam. So a looping recipe renders sample for sample what the same recipe without `loop` renders. A rate cycle's phase after one body moves by `B * inc_P mod 2^64`, a whole number of cycles up to the increment rule's frequency error (Core, Frequency error: under 1e-11 s of cycle period); that error grows smoothly with `n`, like any other phase, and never steps. No sample is stored or replayed, and there is no second clock.

**End at the cap.** The output ends at frame `N` with no fade (Audio, 2026-11-14); past the end every sample is `+0.0` (Stub chain). The click tool leaves the last 50 ms out, as for every session end. Story 4.4 adds the end fade at the cap and the ramp-in inside the opening.

**Seek.** Unchanged (Stub chain, Seek; Rate curve, Seek): seek to frame `k` sets every phase from `k` alone, so a seek into any body pass is byte-identical to a continuous render from frame 0, and the block size cannot change a sample.

**Reference.** The Python reference (`reference/stub_chain.py`) reads `loop`, computes `O`, `B`, `N`, and the seams by the same rules, and renders any frame window `[start, start + T)` straight from the frame numbers, with no change to its formulas: each layer takes the frame indices themselves. Its error bounds are those of the earlier sections at every frame.

**Report.** For a looping recipe the command-line renderer's report gains a member `loop` with `opening_end_frame` (`O`), `seam_frames` (every seam, in order), and `end_frame` (`N`): the seam list a later listening review reads. A report without `loop` is unchanged.

**Fixtures.** `loop-swell-theta-cycle.json` (mode `learn`) is a 450 Hz carrier amplitude-modulated by the sine envelope at a theta rate that cycles over 2 to 6 Hz every 60 s (4 +- 2 Hz from the center, rising), 75 percent depth, and a 250 Hz sine carrier amplitude-modulated at 40 Hz with a smooth swell peaking at the 0 degree coupling phase, 100 percent depth; opening 60 s, body 120 s (two theta rate cycles), 20 min cap, so `length_s` 1200, 9 seams, and a last pass cut at half a body. `loop-bursts-theta-cycle.json` is the same with 50 ms bursts at 0 degrees. `loop-focus-no-cycle.json` (mode `focus`) is the 450 Hz carrier amplitude-modulated by the sine envelope at a fixed 4 Hz theta rate, 75 percent depth, alone; opening 0 s, body 90.25 s (no rate cycle, so any whole-sample body), 45 min cap. The other fixtures of Vectors: loop compile cases are schema-valid and rejected at compile, one per rule and one per rate cycle path. `loop-swell-theta-cycle.json` is the source of the hash probe's `loop-<fs>` components: 5 s from frame 0, then 5 s centered on seam 9 by a seek to `seam 9 - seconds_to_samples(2.5, fs)`.

## Components

Story 3.1 writes the spectral component model: for every v0.1 shape, the spectral lines each layer puts in each ear, in closed form. Compile builds a component table from this section and stores it in the plan; the Python reference builds the same table from this section on its own. The spacing rule of Story 3.2 reads the table. Nothing here changes a sample: the renderer never reads the table, and every fixture, vector, recipe hash, and pinned hash of the earlier sections is unchanged. This section adds no rejection of a recipe that compiles today.

**Line model.** Every theta reference and gamma PAC layer is `a(t) * sin(2 * pi * f_c * t)`: a sine carrier at `f_c` times an amplitude `a >= 0` that repeats. Write `a` as a sum of sinusoids. Each one at frequency `f` and amplitude `2 * A` puts two lines in the layer, at `f_c - f` and `f_c + f`, each of amplitude `A`; the mean of `a` is the amplitude of the line at `f_c`, the carrier line. Since `a >= 0`, no line is stronger than the carrier line. A binaural ear is one unmodulated sine, one line.

**Level.** The level of a line is its amplitude over the amplitude of its layer's carrier line, a linear ratio in (0, 1]. The binaural pair has level 1 in each ear.

**Component.** A component is a line whose level is at least `T = 10^(-L / 20)`, with `L` the value of row `spectral-component-level` in dB: for 40 dB, `T = 0.01`. A line under `T` is not in the table. A line whose ideal level is within row `component-level-error` (1e-6, a ratio) of `T` may be in the table or out of it; no vector below has such a line.

**Rates and cycles.** `f_T` and `f_G` are the theta and gamma carriers, `f_theta` and `f_M` the theta and gamma rates (`rate_hz`, the center with a rate cycle), `f_L` the left binaural carrier and `f_C = carrier_left_hz + beat_rate_hz` (Binaural layer, Oscillators), all binary64 recipe values. For this section a rate has a cycle when its `rate_cycle` (or `beat_rate_cycle`) is present with `swing_hz` above 0; a cycle of swing 0 moves nothing (`K = 0`, so `U = 0`) and counts as no cycle. `s_theta`, `s_M`, and `s_B` are the swings of the theta, gamma, and beat rate cycles, 0 without a cycle.

**Table.** One table per plan, the left ear's rows first, then the right ear's; inside an ear the theta reference rows, then the gamma PAC rows, then the binaural row; inside a layer by ascending `center_hz`, then ascending `gamma_mult`, then ascending `theta_mult`. The theta reference and the gamma PAC layer are the same in both channels, so their rows appear in both ears with the same values. Each row holds:

- `layer`: `theta_reference`, `gamma_pac`, or `binaural`
- `ear`: `left` or `right`
- `center_hz`: the line's frequency at the center rates
- `low_hz`, `high_hz`: the exact interval the line's frequency covers over a whole rate cycle; both equal `center_hz` without a cycle
- `level`: the level defined above
- `gamma_mult`, `theta_mult`, `beat_mult`: integers `i`, `j`, and `b` such that the line's frequency at any moment is `f_c + i * gamma rate(t) + j * theta rate(t) + b * beat rate(t)`, with `f_c` the layer's carrier (`f_L` for both binaural ears) and each rate following its own cycle (Rate curve, Law). Story 3.2 reads them to find where along the cycles two components come closest.

The table depends on the recipe's three layer objects and the muted ear only: never on the sample rate, the session length, the seed, `loop`, or any other field.

**Theta reference.** `d = am_depth_percent / 100.0`. The envelope `e` (Theta reference, Envelope shapes) has Fourier coefficients `c_k`, `e(phi) = sum over k of c_k * exp(2 * pi * i * k * phi)`, with `phi` the fraction of the envelope cycle. The depth law `a = 1 - d * (1 - e)` gives:

```
carrier line (theta_mult 0):   f_T,                  amplitude 1 - d + d * c_0
side lines (theta_mult +-k):   f_T +- k * f_theta,   amplitude d * |c_k|,   k = 1, 2, ...
```

With `h = 1/4` (half the on share, `H / 2^64`) and `w = E * rate_hz` (the edge share of the cycle, the binary64 `w` of Theta reference, Fixed constants):

```
P(k, w) = cos(pi * k * w) / (1 - (2 * k * w)^2),   and pi / 4 where 2 * k * w = 1

sine:              c_0 = 1/2,       |c_1| = 1/4,   c_k = 0 for k >= 2
smoothed_on_off:   c_0 = 2 * h - w, c_k = sin(pi * k * (2 * h - w)) / (pi * k) * P(k, w)
sawtooth:          c_0 = 1/2,       |c_k| = |P(k, w) + sin(pi * k * w) / (pi * k * (1 - w))| / (2 * pi * k)
reverse_saw:       the sawtooth's c_0 and |c_k| (a mirror image has the same line amplitudes)
```

`P(k, w)` is the transform of one raised-cosine edge: the smoothed on-off envelope is a rectangle of width `2 * h - w` (edge midpoint to edge midpoint) smoothed by the edge. At depth 0 every side line is 0 and the layer is the carrier alone. For the sine shape the levels are `(d / 4) / (1 - d / 2)`: 0.3 at 75 percent.

**Gamma PAC layer, uncoupled.** `a = 1 - d * (1 - e)` with the sine envelope at the gamma rate:

```
carrier line (0, 0):     f_G,          amplitude 1 - d / 2
side lines (+-1, 0):     f_G +- f_M,   amplitude d / 4
```

Rows are named `(gamma_mult, theta_mult)`.

**Gamma PAC layer, smooth swell.** `a = 1 - (d * s) * (1 - e)` with `s` and `1 - e` each one raised cosine (Coupling, Swell law), so the product has nine lines:

```
(0, 0):       f_G,                     amplitude 1 - d / 4
(0, +-1):     f_G +- f_theta,          amplitude d / 8
(+-1, 0):     f_G +- f_M,              amplitude d / 8
(+-1, +-1):   f_G +- f_M +- f_theta,   amplitude d / 16      (all four sign pairs)
```

The coupling phase turns the phases of these lines and moves none of them, so it changes no row. At 100 percent the levels are 1/6 and 1/12. Inside the locked ranges (gamma rate at least 30 Hz, theta rate at most 6 Hz) no two of the nine lines share a frequency.

**Gamma PAC layer, bursts.** `a = 1 - (d * g) * (1 - e)`. The burst gate `g` (Bursts, Burst gate) is the smoothed on-off form about the coupling phase, so its coefficients `g_j` are the smoothed on-off `c_j` above with `h = w_H` and `w = w_W`, the binary64 products `0.025 * rate_hz` and `0.01 * rate_hz` of Bursts, Fixed constants, at the theta reference's `rate_hz`; `g_-j = g_j`, and `g_0 = 2 * h - w`. Each gate harmonic `j` carries the phase factor `z^j`, `z = exp(-2 * pi * i * C / 2^64)`, with `C` the coupling phase in phase units (Coupling, Coupling phase rule). The lines `(i, j)` lie at `f_G + i * f_M + j * f_theta` for `i` in {-1, 0, 1} and every integer `j`, with complex amplitudes:

```
(0, 0):               1 - d * g_0 / 2
(0, j), j != 0:       -(d / 2) * g_j * z^j
(+-1, j), every j:    (d / 4) * g_j * z^j
```

A line that shares its frequency with no other has the magnitude of its amplitude: `d * |g_j| / 2` for `(0, j)` and `d * |g_j| / 4` for `(+-1, j)`.

**Bursts, lines at one frequency.** When neither the theta rate nor the gamma rate has a cycle, lines at the same frequency add as complex amplitudes and the table holds one row with the magnitude of the sum. Two burst lines share a frequency exactly when twice the gamma rate is a whole multiple of the theta rate. The test is the Cycle rule's (Loop), in binary64:

```
q = (2.0 * f_M) / f_theta      the doubling exact, the division one rounding
whole when 0 < q < 2^30, q is an integer, and q * f_theta == 2.0 * f_M (one rounding); then n2 = q
```

The cap `2^30` keeps `n2` a 32-bit integer; inside the locked ranges `q` is at most 120 (2 times 60 Hz over 1 Hz), so it never decides a recipe.

- **`n2` even (the gamma rate is a whole multiple `n = n2 / 2` of the theta rate, for example 60 Hz over 6 Hz):** every line lies at `f_G + m * f_theta` for an integer `m` and sums three members, `(0, m)`, `(1, m - n)`, and `(-1, m + n)`. The carrier line, `m = 0`, sums to `1 - d * g_0 / 2 + (d / 2) * g_n * cos(2 * pi * n * C / 2^64)`. The row is named `(0, m)`.
- **`n2` odd (for example 45 Hz over 6 Hz, `n2 = 15`):** the lines `(0, j)` stand alone. The line at `f_G + f_M + j * f_theta` sums two members, `(1, j)` and `(-1, j + n2)`. The row is named after the member with the smaller `|theta_mult|` (they never tie, since `n2` is odd).
- **Not whole:** no two lines share a frequency and every line stands alone.

Levels are then the summed magnitudes over the summed carrier line. The binary64 test treats rates such as 57 Hz over 5.7 Hz as whole although 10 times the binary64 nearest 5.7 is not exactly 57: the members then lie within 1e-13 Hz of each other, far closer than any session can resolve. With a cycle on either rate the members sweep apart and every line is listed on its own, also in the special case where both cycles keep the ratio whole at every moment. Without a `theta_reference` field, or at gamma depth 0 (the only coupled recipes compile accepts without theta AM depth), the layer is the carrier alone, for the swell and for bursts.

**Binaural layer.** One row per ear, level 1, `gamma_mult` and `theta_mult` 0:

```
left:    f_L,   beat_mult 0
right:   f_C,   beat_mult 1,   interval [f_C - s_B, f_C + s_B]
```

A muted ear (Binaural layer, Muted ear) has no binaural row; the other ear keeps its row. Without a `binaural` field there is no binaural row.

**Rate cycles.** Along a rate cycle the theta envelope, the swell, and the burst gate keep their shape as functions of the theta envelope phase (`W`, `Hb`, and `Wb` are fixed shares of the theta cycle, Rate curve), and the gamma envelope keeps its shape as a function of its phase. So no coefficient changes: every level is the level at the center rates, with `w` and `h` at the center `rate_hz`. Each line sweeps in frequency and keeps its power. A row `(i, j)` has `center_hz = f_c + i * f_M + j * f_theta` and moves by `i * s_M * sin(2 * pi * t / P + phi_M) + j * s_theta * sin(2 * pi * t / P + phi_theta)`, with `phi_M` and `phi_theta` the start phases of the gamma and theta cycles. Both periods are equal (row `rate-cycle-period` is one value), so the two terms add to one sinusoid of amplitude

```
half = sqrt((i * s_M)^2 + (j * s_theta)^2 + 2 * (i * s_M) * (j * s_theta) * cos(phi_M - phi_theta))
low_hz = center_hz - half,   high_hz = center_hz + half
```

With one cycle, or `i = 0` or `j = 0`, `half` is `|i| * s_M + |j| * s_theta`. The interval is the range over a whole cycle whatever the session length, so it is a closed-form worst case and the session is never sampled. A story that unlocks the period must extend this paragraph first.

**Frequencies below 0.** A line whose frequency is below 0 sounds at its absolute value. A row whose interval lies at or below 0 is listed mirrored: `center_hz`, `low_hz`, and `high_hz` become `-center_hz`, `-high_hz`, and `-low_hz`. A row whose interval crosses 0 is listed as `|center_hz|`, `low_hz = 0`, and `high_hz` the larger of `-low_hz` and `high_hz`. The multipliers keep describing the unmirrored frequency. A mirrored line is listed on its own even where it lands on another line of its layer. Inside the locked ranges this happens only to weak outer lines of a theta reference with a carrier near 100 Hz (for example a 100 Hz carrier amplitude-modulated at 6 Hz by the smoothed on-off envelope at 100 percent depth, whose line `theta_mult = -17` lies at 2 Hz).

**Search bound.** The coefficient series never end, so the builder needs to know where no further component can lie. With

```
Pb(k, w) = 1 where (2 * k * w)^2 <= 2, else 1 / ((2 * k * w)^2 - 1)        |P(k, w)| <= Pb(k, w)
smoothed on-off and burst gate:   |c_k| <= B(k) = Pb(k, w) / (pi * k)
sawtooth and reverse saw:         |c_k| <= B(k) = (Pb(k, w) + 1 / (pi * k * (1 - w))) / (2 * pi * k)
```

`B(k)` never rises with `k`. So for a theta shape, once `d * B(k) < T * (1 - d + d * c_0)`, no line `k` or beyond is a component. For bursts with lines that stand alone, once `(d / 2) * B(j) < T * (1 - d * g_0 / 2)`, no line with `|theta_mult| >= j` is. For summed lines each sum has at most three members of total weight `d`: with `J` the first `j` where `d * B(j)` is under `T` times the summed carrier amplitude, no row beyond `|m| = n + J` (`n2` even), and no summed row whose two members both have `|theta_mult| >= J` (`n2` odd), is a component. The engine and the reference each stop by these bounds; neither samples a signal or takes a transform.

**Unmodeled shape.** Compile rejects every shape this file does not model before the table is built, with the texts and in the order of the earlier sections (Theta reference: `shape not modeled in the signal spec`; Gamma PAC layer: `non-sine carrier not modeled`; Binaural layer: `non-sine binaural not modeled`). The table builder also refuses on its own: given a recipe with a custom theta shape, a non-sine gamma carrier wave, or a non-sine binaural waveform, it returns no table and an error with rule `no component model`, the parameter, value, and limit of the matching compile rule (`/theta_reference/shape`, `/gamma_pac/carrier_wave`, `/binaural/waveform`; checked in that order). A shape added later has no table until this section models it.

**Carrier rule.** The carrier rule (row `carrier-rule`) is unchanged by this section. Its modulation rate is the layer's own named rate: the theta rate for the theta reference and the gamma rate for the gamma PAC layer, each at its highest value along a rate cycle (Rate curve, Range checks). Coupling does not change it: the swell's lines at `f_G +- f_M +- f_theta` and the burst gate's harmonics are components of this table, read by the spacing rule, and are not modulation rates for the carrier rule. This is an engineering default; no source sets a carrier-to-rate ratio for a carrier with nested AM (design log 2026-11-16 (vv), independent review of 2026-11-16).

**Engine.** The builder uses binary64 addition, subtraction, multiplication, division, comparison, and square root (each one correctly rounded IEEE 754 operation), integer arithmetic, and the cycle cosine (Rate curve, Cycle cosine). It makes no transcendental library call, so the table has the same bytes on every platform; the hash probe component `components` checks that on all four.

```
T        the binary64 nearest 10^(-L / 20): 0x1.47ae147ae147bp-7 for 40 dB
pi       0x1.921fb54442d18p+1
cos2pi(x) = cycle_cosine(cycles-to-units rule (x))                       cos(2 * pi * x), x in cycles
sin2pi(x) = cycle_cosine(cycles-to-units rule (x) - 2^62 mod 2^64)       sin(2 * pi * x)
sin(pi * y) = sin2pi(y * 0.5),   cos(pi * y) = cos2pi(y * 0.5)
z^j:  real part cycle_cosine(j * C mod 2^64), imaginary part -cycle_cosine(j * C - 2^62 mod 2^64)
cos(phi_M - phi_theta) = cycle_cosine(P0_M - P0_theta mod 2^64),  each P0 by the coupling phase rule
```

`P(k, w)` loses every digit near `2 * k * w = 1` if divided as written, so with `x = 2 * k * w` and `delta = x - 1`:

```
|delta| >= 2^-5:   P = cos(pi * k * w) / (1 - x * x)
|delta| <  2^-5:   y = (pi / 2) * delta
                   S = 1 - y^2 / 6 + y^4 / 120 - y^6 / 5040          sin(y) / y, within 1e-16
                   P = (pi / 2) * S / (2 + delta)
```

Both forms are the same function (`cos(pi * x / 2) = -sin(pi * delta / 2)` and `1 - x^2 = -delta * (2 + delta)`), and the second gives `pi / 4` at `delta = 0`. `center_hz` is `f_c + (i * f_M + j * f_theta)` in binary64, and `low_hz` and `high_hz` are `center_hz -+ half`. The vectors do not fix the engine's bits: the cycle cosine is within 1e-10 of the ideal cosine (row `cycle-cosine-error`), which keeps every level within about 1e-8 of its ideal, and the two rows below bound the result.

**Error rows.** Against the vectors below, every `level` of the engine table and of the reference table is within row `component-level-error` (1e-6, a ratio), and every `center_hz`, `low_hz`, and `high_hz` within row `component-frequency-error` (1e-9 Hz). Both tables have exactly the vector rows: the same layers, ears, and multipliers, in the same order. Two rows of one layer can have equal ideal centers only with a rate cycle (their intervals then differ); a binary64 table orders such a pair by its own rounded centers, and in every vector case those centers are exact, so the multipliers decide. Both rows are engineering defaults (design log 2026-11-16 (vv)).

**Reference.** The Python reference (`reference/components.py`, implemented from this section, never from engine code or engine output) builds the same table in binary64 with NumPy's sine, cosine, and square root, the same `delta` form of `P(k, w)` near `2 * k * w = 1`, and the same search bounds.

**Cross-check.** The spectrum tool (`tests/assertions/spectrum.py`, unchanged) measures reference renders of the Epic 2 fixtures, one layer tap at a time, and the measurement is held to the table. A fixture without a rate cycle: every measured line within `L` dB of its tap's strongest line is a table row within rows `spectrum-frequency` and `spectrum-level`, and every table row is measured. A fixture with a rate cycle (120 s, two whole cycles): the table's intervals, each widened by the tool's resolution (`8 * fs / N` Hz for `N` samples, the window's main lobe) plus the row's sweep edge, merge into bands; the measured power of each band equals the sum of its rows' `level^2` within row `spectrum-level`, and no line at or above the threshold lies outside the bands. A swept line keeps its power, so a band is judged by power over whole cycles and not by peak height. The band power measure is calibrated first on Python-synthesized swept tones of known power. A table doctored in the test harness to drop one side frequency fails the cross-check.

**Sweep edge.** A line swept sinusoidally over `[center_hz - A, center_hz + A]` with period `P` (row `rate-cycle-period`) is not a line that stops at `center_hz +- A`: it is the Bessel line spectrum of modulation index `beta = A * P`, lines `1 / P` apart at `center_hz + n / P` with amplitudes `|J_n(beta)|`, and near the turning points those lines reach past `A`. The sweep edge of a row is the smallest `d >= 0` such that every line with `n > (A + d) * P` is below the threshold `T` re that row's strongest Bessel line: with `n_last` the largest `|n|` where `|J_n(beta)| >= T * max |J_n(beta)|`, `d = n_last / P - A`. `A = (high_hz - low_hz) / 2`; a static row has `d = 0`. It is closed form, depends on `A`, `P`, and row `spectral-component-level` only, and is never tuned to a fixture (the cross-check tool computes `J_n(beta)` as the FFT of `exp(i beta sin t)` over one period, sampled finely enough that no line aliases). At the 60 s period it is, for example, 0.18 Hz for the Sleep beat rate cycle (`A` 1.5 Hz) and 0.38 Hz for a 15 Hz gamma rate swing. The table's intervals are unchanged; the edge belongs to the measurement.

### Vectors: component coefficients

`P(k, w)`, the coefficient, and its bound `B(k)` (25 significant digits, decimal arithmetic) per form, theta rate, and `k`. The coefficient is the signed `c_k` for the smoothed on-off envelope (`h = 1/4`) and the signed `g_k` for the burst gate (`h = 0.025 * rate`, `w = 0.01 * rate`), and `|c_k|` for the sawtooth, which the reverse saw shares. `h` and `w` are the binary64 values; `none` marks a column that does not apply (`h` for the sawtooth, `P` and `B` at `k = 0`). At 5 Hz and `k = 10`, `2 * k * w` is within one rounding of 1.

### Vectors: component cases

The recipes of the component vectors: every Epic 2 shape fixture (the case is named after the file in `recipes/fixtures/valid/`, and its three layer objects equal the file's), then cases no fixture has. Each layer cell is the layer object as the recipe writes it, `none` when absent; `Muted ear` is the compile option. `Components` is the table's row count, both ears. The vectors file also holds a complete recipe text per case (10 s, the case's layers and nothing else that could change the table), from which a test compiles the cases without a fixture. The cases `theta-smoothed-on-off-1hz`, `theta-sawtooth-6hz`, `bursts-45hz`, `bursts-60hz`, `gamma-cycle-swell-theta-cycle`, and `binaural-cycle-sleep`, in this order, are the hash probe's `components` source.

## Spacing

Story 3.2 writes the spacing rule. Compile reads the component table (Components) and checks, in each ear, the distance between every component of one layer and every component of another, over the whole session in closed form. A recipe that fails returns no plan. Nothing here changes a sample, a component row, or any vector, fixture byte, recipe hash, or pinned hash of the earlier sections. A recipe with fewer than two layers in each ear meets no pair, so its compile result and its report bytes are unchanged.

**Rule.** In each ear, every component row of one layer must stay at least `S` from every component row of another layer at every moment, with `S` the value of row `spacing-rule` (50 Hz, a placeholder: design log 2026-10-27 (e)). The pairs are the theta reference against the gamma PAC layer, the theta reference against the binaural layer, and the gamma PAC layer against the binaural layer, each in the ear the two rows share. A layer's own rows are never compared with one another. The two binaural rows are in different ears, so they are never compared; each still counts against the other layers in its own ear. A muted ear has no binaural row (Components, Binaural layer), so it has no binaural pair. A pair passes when its minimum distance is at least `S`, compared in binary64: exactly 50 Hz passes, anything less fails.

**Table index.** The index of a row is its position in the whole table of the plan (Components, Table): the left ear's rows first from 0, then the right ear's. In a pair, row 1 is the row with the smaller index and row 2 the other. Since the table orders the layers theta reference, gamma PAC layer, binaural, row 2's layer is the later one in field order.

**Distance of two rows.** By its multipliers (Components, Table) a row's frequency at time `t` is its `center_hz` plus `i * s_M * sin(theta + phi_M) + j * s_theta * sin(theta + phi_theta) + b * s_B * sin(theta + phi_B)`, with `theta = 2 * pi * t / P` and every cycle on the one period `P` of row `rate-cycle-period` (Rate curve, Law; Components, Rate cycles). `s_M`, `s_theta`, and `s_B` are the swings of the gamma, theta, and beat rate cycles (0 without a cycle, or with swing 0), and `phi_M`, `phi_theta`, `phi_B` their start phases, `2 * pi * P0 / 2^64` with `P0` by the coupling phase rule of the cycle's `start_phase_deg`. For two rows of one ear, neither listed mirrored (below), the frequency of row 1 less that of row 2 is

```
D(t) = c + X * sin(theta) + Y * cos(theta)
c          = center_hz(1) - center_hz(2)
di, dj, db = gamma_mult(1) - gamma_mult(2), theta_mult(1) - theta_mult(2), beat_mult(1) - beat_mult(2)
a = di * s_M,   b = dj * s_theta,   e = db * s_B
X = a * cos(phi_M) + b * cos(phi_theta) + e * cos(phi_B)
Y = a * sin(phi_M) + b * sin(phi_theta) + e * sin(phi_B)
H = sqrt(X^2 + Y^2)
```

so `D` is one sinusoid of amplitude `H` about `c`, and

```
H^2 = a^2 + b^2 + e^2 + 2 a b cos(phi_M - phi_theta) + 2 a e cos(phi_M - phi_B) + 2 b e cos(phi_theta - phi_B)
minimum distance = max(0, |c| - H)
```

This is the half-width law of Components, Rate cycles, extended to three terms and applied to the difference of the two rows' multipliers. It is the minimum of `|D(t)|` over a whole cycle, whatever the session length, so a collision at any instant of the session is found and the session is never sampled. Two rows that sweep together are judged by their real distance, not by their intervals: a theta reference row and a gamma PAC row with the same `theta_mult` on the same theta cycle have `dj = 0`, and the theta cycle adds nothing to `H`. A rate without a cycle adds no term, whatever the multiplier on it.

**Rows mirrored below 0 Hz.** A row is listed mirrored when its unmirrored interval reaches below 0 Hz (`center - half < 0` before the mirror, Components, Frequencies below 0). A pair with such a row is compared by the two listed intervals at their worst case, `max(0, low_hz(2) - high_hz(1), low_hz(1) - high_hz(2))`. Inside the locked ranges only weak outer lines of a theta reference with a carrier near 100 Hz are mirrored (Components, Frequencies below 0).

**Where the minimum occurs.** A row sweeps when it has a nonzero multiplier on a rate whose cycle has a swing above 0. The detail of a spacing error names where the minimum occurs:

- `whole session` when neither row sweeps: the distance is the same at every moment.
- `listed intervals, a row mirrored below 0 Hz` when a row is mirrored and a row sweeps: the worst case of two intervals has no single moment.
- Otherwise the first time `t` in `[0, P)` from the session start at which `|D(t)|` is at its minimum, and the gamma, theta, and beat rates at that time (each rate by Rate curve, Law; `none` for a layer the recipe does not have). With `H = 0` the distance never changes and the time is 0 s. With `|c| >= H` the minimum is at the one turning point of `D` each cycle that lies nearest 0. With `|c| < H` the distance is 0 at two times each cycle, and the first of them is given. The time is in the first cycle whatever the session length, since the rule judges the whole cycle; a session shorter than `P` may end before it.

**Which failure.** Among all failing pairs in both ears, compile reports the one with the smallest minimum distance. Ties go to the left ear first, then to the smaller index of row 1, then of row 2.

**Error.** Rule `spacing_rule`. Parameter: the carrier field of row 2's layer, `/gamma_pac/carrier_hz` or `/binaural/carrier_left_hz`. Value: the minimum distance, as the shortest round-trip text of its binary64 (as every range error). Limit: `>= 50 Hz (row spacing-rule)`, the row value written the same way. Detail:

```
<ear> ear: <layer 1> (<i>, <j>, <b>) at <center 1> Hz and <layer 2> (<i>, <j>, <b>) at <center 2> Hz; <where>
<where>:  whole session
       |  listed intervals, a row mirrored below 0 Hz
       |  first at <t> s, gamma rate <rate> Hz, theta rate <rate> Hz, beat rate <rate> Hz
```

`<ear>` is `left` or `right`; each layer is written as in the table (`theta_reference`, `gamma_pac`, `binaural`) with its row's `gamma_mult`, `theta_mult`, and `beat_mult`; each center is the row's `center_hz`; a rate of a missing layer is written `none` in place of `<rate> Hz`. Every number is the shortest round-trip text of a binary64. Where a tool prints the error on one line (the command-line renderer's and the tap dump's `rejected: parameter=... value=... rule=... limit=... detail=...`), `detail=` is always the last field and runs to the end of the line. For example the must-fail fixture gives parameter `/binaural/carrier_left_hz`, value `4`, rule `spacing_rule`, limit `>= 50 Hz (row spacing-rule)`, detail `right ear: gamma_pac (-1, 0, 0) at 260 Hz and binaural (0, 0, 1) at 256 Hz; whole session`. Every other rule leaves the detail empty.

**Compile order.** The spacing rule runs once the component table is built, so after every earlier compile rule (the range checks with the loop rules, the carrier rules, the shape and carrier wave rules, the coupling rules, and the binaural waveform rule), and before `final.required`, in both compile modes. A rejected recipe has no plan, so the command-line renderer writes no WAV.

**Margins.** A plan keeps, per ear, the pair with the smallest minimum distance (ties to the smaller index of row 1, then of row 2), its minimum distance, its margin (the minimum distance less `S`, one binary64 subtraction, 0 or more in a plan that compiled), and the two row indices. An ear with fewer than two layers has no pair and no margin.

**Report.** The command-line renderer's report gains a member `spacing`, after `loop` (or after `frames` without one) and before `determinism_key`, written only when at least one ear has a pair, so every other report is unchanged byte for byte:

```
  "spacing": {
    "left": {"min_distance_hz": 118, "margin_hz": 68, "component_1": 0, "component_2": 35, "pair": "theta_reference (0, -1, 0) at 444 Hz and gamma_pac (1, 6, 0) at 326 Hz"},
    "right": {"min_distance_hz": 118, "margin_hz": 68, "component_1": 36, "component_2": 71, "pair": "theta_reference (0, -1, 0) at 444 Hz and gamma_pac (1, 6, 0) at 326 Hz"}
  },
```

Each ear is an object, or `null` without a pair. The numbers are written as every report number is (shortest round-trip text); `pair` is the first part of the detail grammar, without the ear.

**Engine.** The rule uses binary64 addition, subtraction, multiplication, division, comparison, and square root, integer arithmetic, and the cycle cosine (Rate curve, Cycle cosine). It makes no transcendental library call. Once per plan, for each rate with a cycle of swing above 0:

```
P0      = coupling phase rule (start_phase_deg)
cos_phi = cycle_cosine(P0)
sin_phi = cycle_cosine(P0 - 2^62 mod 2^64)
```

and `s = 0`, `cos_phi = sin_phi = 0.0` for a rate without one, or for a layer the recipe does not have. Per pair, in this order:

```
a    = double(di) * s_M,   b = double(dj) * s_theta,   e = double(db) * s_B     one rounding each
X    = (a * cos_M + b * cos_theta) + e * cos_B
Y    = (a * sin_M + b * sin_theta) + e * sin_B
H    = sqrt(X * X + Y * Y)
c    = center_hz(1) - center_hz(2)
dist = |c| - H, and +0.0 where that is below 0
```

A pair with a mirrored row has `dist = max(+0.0, low_hz(2) - high_hz(1), low_hz(1) - high_hz(2))`, each subtraction one rounding. The pair passes when `dist >= S`. Evaluating `H` as `sqrt(X^2 + Y^2)` keeps the error of the cycle cosine linear in the swings (about 1e-10 Hz per pair inside the locked ranges); the three-term law with `cos(phi_M - phi_theta)` is the same value but loses digits where its terms cancel.

The time of the failing pair is found by integer bisection on the cycle phase. `acos_units(v)` is the smallest integer `x` in `[0, 2^63]` with `cycle_cosine(x) <= v`:

```
acos_units(v):
  if cycle_cosine(0) <= v: return 0
  lo = 0, hi = 2^63
  while hi - lo > 1:
    mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2                  integer
    if cycle_cosine(mid) <= v: hi = mid  else: lo = mid
  return hi
```

With `u` the cycle phase advance from the session start:

```
if neither row sweeps:  where = whole session
else if a row is mirrored:  where = listed intervals, a row mirrored below 0 Hz
else if H == 0:  u = 0
else:
  sigma = 1.0 if c >= 0 else -1.0             (c = -0.0 counts as >= 0)
  alpha = acos_units((-sigma * Y) / H)        the turning point of D nearest 0: cos(theta) = -sigma Y / H
  m     = alpha if -sigma * X >= 0 else 2^64 - alpha mod 2^64      and sin(theta) = -sigma X / H
  if |c| >= H:  u = m
  else:
    beta = acos_units(|c| / H)               D is 0 at m - beta and m + beta
    u    = min(m - beta mod 2^64, m + beta mod 2^64)
t    = (double(u) * 0x1p-64) * period_s
rate = rate_hz + s * cycle_cosine(P0 + u - 2^62 mod 2^64)       per rate with a cycle; rate_hz without one
```

`period_s` is the period of any cycle of the pair (all are row `rate-cycle-period`). Only the reported failure needs a time, so a compile makes at most two bisections of 63 steps each.

**Error rows.** Against the vectors below, the engine's and the reference's minimum distances and margins are within row `component-frequency-error` (1e-9 Hz); the vector generator runs the binary64 steps above on every pair of every case and holds each within that row (worst 1.4e-10 Hz). Each first time is within row `spacing-time-error` (1 ms), measured around the cycle (a time within it of `P` is within it of 0 s); the cycle cosine's error moves a bisected angle by at most about `sqrt(2 * e_c)` radians, under 1.4e-4 s at the 60 s period, and the generator's run of these steps is within 1e-6 s. Each rate at that time is within row `frequency-accuracy` (0.01 Hz): a rate moves at most `2 * pi * s / P`, under 1.6 Hz per second at the widest swing, so 1 ms moves it under 0.002 Hz. Each center in the detail is within row `component-frequency-error`. Three conditions no vector meets: a pair whose ideal minimum distance is within row `component-frequency-error` of `S` may pass or fail, apart from the exact cases (two static rows whose centers differ by exactly `S` in binary64, as in `spacing-exactly-50hz`); a zero of `D` within row `spacing-time-error` of the cycle start may be reported or passed over for the other zero; and a pair whose ideal `H` is not 0 but under 1e-6 Hz has a time that may lie anywhere, since its distance changes by less than that over the cycle. `spacing-time-error` is an engineering default (design log 2026-11-16 (ww)).

**Reference.** The Python reference (`reference/spacing.py`, implemented from this section, never from engine code or engine output) computes every distance, margin, failure, and detail on its own from its own component table (`reference/components.py`), in binary64 with NumPy's sine, cosine, arc cosine, and square root. It may find the time in closed form; it must agree with the error rows above.

**Fixtures.** `starter-focus-layers.json`, `starter-learn-layers.json`, `starter-relax-layers.json`, and `starter-sleep-layers.json` are the layer configurations of the four starters (`starter-recipes.md`; design log 2026-10-27 (m), (n)): Focus, a 450 Hz carrier amplitude-modulated by the sine envelope at 6 Hz, 75 percent depth, and a 250 Hz sine carrier amplitude-modulated at 40 Hz with 50 ms bursts at the 0 degree coupling phase, 100 percent depth, looping at its 45 min cap; Learn, the same theta reference at 5.5 Hz with a smooth swell peaking at 0 degrees, 100 percent depth, looping at its 20 min cap; Relax, the theta reference at 6 Hz with a smooth swell at 0 degrees, 25 percent depth, 30 min; Sleep, the theta reference at a steady 2 Hz and a binaural layer with a 100 Hz left carrier and a beat rate that cycles over 0 to 3 Hz every 60 s from 0 Hz rising, 45 min. Focus and Learn open with 60 s and loop a 120 s body. None has a ramp, a fade, or a level field (Epic 4), and none is an Epic 6 starter recipe. Story 3.3 renders its previews from them. `spacing-binaural-250-256-gamma-260.json` is the must-fail fixture: a 300 Hz sine carrier amplitude-modulated at 40 Hz, uncoupled, 100 percent depth (lines at 260, 300, and 340 Hz), beside a binaural layer of a 250 Hz left carrier and a 256 Hz right carrier, 10 s. Its worst pair is in the right ear, 4 Hz apart.

**Existing recipes.** Every fixture in `recipes/fixtures/valid/` that compiled before this story still compiles: none of them has a pair closer than 64 Hz (Vectors: spacing margins). The coupled gamma plus binaural recipes of the AD-5 sweep (`tests/python/test_binaural_ad5.py`, cases `ad5-*` below) pass with at least 14 Hz to spare. The must-fail fixture is the only fixture this section names as a collision.

**Carrier rule.** The carrier rule (row `carrier-rule`) still reads the recipe fields, unchanged (Components, Carrier rule). AD-20's "one table" is read this way: the spacing rule, the tests, and the carrier guard read the component table, and the carrier rule's input is the layer's named rate, which the table's multipliers name (design log 2026-11-16 (ww)).

**Vectors.** The cases are every component case (Vectors: component cases), the Story 3.2 fixtures, the fourteen AD-5 sweep recipes, and cases no fixture has: exactly 50 Hz (`spacing-exactly-50hz`, a 450 Hz carrier at 0 percent depth beside a 400 Hz binaural pair with beat 0), 49.99 Hz (`spacing-49.99hz`, the binaural pair at 400.01 Hz), a beat rate cycle that comes within 50 Hz only at its turning point (`spacing-turning-point`, beat rate 3 Hz at 30 s), a gamma rate cycle whose line crosses the left binaural carrier (`spacing-gamma-cycle-crossing`, first at gamma rate 50 Hz), lines that sweep together (`spacing-sweep-together`: their intervals are 46.5 Hz apart, their distance is 51.5 Hz at every moment), cycles 60 degrees apart (`spacing-cycles-60deg-apart`), all three cycles (`spacing-three-cycles`), a mirrored row (`spacing-mirrored-row`), and one layer (`spacing-one-layer`). The vectors file also holds a complete recipe text per case, as for the component cases. Values are ideal, at 25 significant digits, from the generator's decimal arithmetic: centers as in the component vectors, cosines and sines of the start phases in decimal (exact at the quarter points), and each first time by exact bisection on the cycle fraction. A detail's numbers are the ideal values; the engine writes its own binary64 values, so a test reads the detail by its grammar and compares the words exactly and the numbers within the error rows. The pair vectors give the closed form on its own, with the first cycle fraction `t / P` in place of the time.

### Vectors: spacing cases

Every spacing case: the component cases first (by their names in Vectors: component cases), then the Story 3.2 fixtures, the AD-5 sweep recipes (`ad5-swell-*` and `ad5-bursts-*`, the seven binaural settings of `tests/python/test_binaural_ad5.py` beside the swell or the burst gamma PAC layer), and the cases no fixture has. Each layer cell is the layer object as the recipe writes it, `none` when absent; `Components` is the component row count, both ears. A rejected case gives the error: the value is the ideal minimum distance and the detail holds ideal numbers (Vectors, above). `none` marks a field of a case that passes.

## Preview

Story 3.3 writes the listening preview: a short, quiet render of a recipe that the command-line renderer makes with `--preview`, so a harsh carrier or a rough gamma pulse can be heard long before golden sign-off. It is not an engine stage. The engine compiles and renders the recipe exactly as it does without `--preview`: the plan, the recipe hash, the component table, the spacing margins, the loop frames, and every tap are the recipe's own. The renderer keeps the first `F` frames of that render and scales each output sample by one gain `g` before 24-bit rounding. Nothing here changes a sample, a vector, a fixture byte, a recipe hash, a pinned hash, or any render or report made without `--preview`. A preview is never a golden and is never signed; no hash probe component and nothing in `ci/` reads one.

**Rows.** Three threshold rows, engineering defaults for previews only (design log 2026-11-17 (xx)): `preview-length` (180 s, the "about 3 minutes" of the story), `preview-seam-tail` (30 s heard past the first body seam of a looping recipe), and `preview-peak-level` (`P`, -30 dBFS, the analytic peak of the summed layers). No study sets them.

**Mode and rate.** A preview is made in measure mode only: `--preview` with `--mode final` is a usage error (exit 2) and writes nothing. Any sample rate the engine accepts may be used; the listening previews are made at 48 kHz.

**Frames.** Once per render, with the seconds-to-samples rule (Core) and exact integer arithmetic:

```
N = seconds_to_samples(length_s, fs)              the session end (Stub chain; Loop, Frames)
L = seconds_to_samples(preview_length_s, fs)      row preview-length
T = seconds_to_samples(preview_seam_tail_s, fs)   row preview-seam-tail
no loop:   F = min(L, N)
loop:      F = min(O + B + T, N)                  O and B as in Loop, Frames
```

So a recipe without a loop previews its first `min(180 s, length_s)`, and a looping recipe previews its opening, the join at `O` (the seam from the opening into the first body pass), one whole body, the first body seam at `O + B`, and `T` frames past it: for Focus and Learn, 60 + 120 + 30 = 210 s. When a looping session holds only one body pass (`O + B = N`, no seam), `F = N`. The preview is frames `[0, F)` of the full render, sample for sample: no seek, no reset, and no frame past `F` is rendered.

**Layers.** Every layer runs at unit amplitude (the Level paragraphs of Stub chain, Gamma PAC layer, Coupling, Bursts, and Binaural layer): the theta reference tap, the gamma PAC tap, and each binaural sine are each at most 1 in magnitude, and the per-channel gain is 1.0 and the limiter stub the identity (Stub chain). So in one ear the output is at most `n`, the number of layers present in that ear: 1 for `theta_reference`, 1 for `gamma_pac` (both are the same in both ears), and 1 for `binaural` in each ear that is not muted. `n` is the analytic peak; the samples stay at or below it, and below it wherever the layers' peaks never meet. `n_max` is the larger of the two ears' counts. A layer present in an ear has its carrier row in that ear's component table (Components, Table), and a muted ear has no binaural row, so counting the layers of each ear's rows (`arc_plan_component`) gives the same `n`. The command-line renderer never mutes an ear; the count still honors a plan's muted ear.

**Gain.** `g = 10^(P / 20) / n_max`, with `P` the value of row `preview-peak-level`, and an `n_max` of 0 (no layer: a silent render) counted as 1. So the summed layers of the louder ear peak at `P` dBFS at most, and an ear with fewer layers lower. To give `g` the same bits on every platform with no transcendental call, `P` must be a whole multiple of 10 dB from -440 to 0 dB: then `10^(P / 20)` is a power of ten, or a power of ten times `sqrt(10)`, and 10^22 is the largest power of ten binary64 holds exactly. Any other value of the row (for example -25, -30.5, -0.5, 10, or -450) is a table error, found when the row is read and before any output is opened: the renderer exits 3 and writes nothing. In binary64, each step one correctly rounded operation in this order:

```
valid when -440.0 <= P <= 0.0, m = P / 10.0 is an integer (the Whole-sample rule's integer test, Loop), and m * 10.0 == P
k = -m, as an integer (0 to 44)
x = 1.0
repeat k div 2 times:  x = x * 10.0             exact: 10^j for j <= 22
if k is odd:           x = x * sqrt(10.0)       one correctly rounded square root, 0x1.94c583ada5b53p+1, then one rounding
a = 1.0 / x                                     one rounding: 10^(P / 20)
g = a / double(max(n_max, 1))                   one rounding
```

For -30 dB, `k = 3`: `x = 10.0 * sqrt(10.0)`, `a = 1 / (10 * sqrt(10))`, and with two layers `g = a / 2.0` (an exact halving), `0x1.030dc4ea03a72p-6`, about 0.0158114. The steps use only multiplication, division, comparison, and square root (AD-9), so `g` has the same bits on every platform; `g` is within 2 units in the last place of the exact value.

**Per sample.** For `0 <= n < F` and each channel `c`, with `out[c][n]` the render output (Stub chain, Output and taps):

```
w[c][n] = out[c][n] * g      binary64, one rounding
```

and `w` is written by the 24-bit rounding rule (24-bit rounding) in place of `out`. A NaN stays NaN and is written as 0, as without a preview. The WAV is the same layout as every render (2 channels, 24 bits, the RIFF or RF64 header) for `F` frames.

**Report.** A preview report has `frames` equal to `F`. The `loop` and `spacing` members are the plan's own, unchanged: `seam_frames` lists every seam of the recipe up to `N`, including the seams past `F`. A member `preview` follows `spacing` (or `loop` without a spacing block, or `frames` without either) and comes before `determinism_key`:

```
  "preview": {
    "signed": false,
    "peak_dbfs": -30,
    "layers_per_ear": 2,
    "gain": 0.015811388300841896,
    "seconds": 210
  },
```

`signed` is always `false`: no preview is signed off (golden sign-off is Epic 6). `peak_dbfs` is `P`, `layers_per_ear` is `n_max` (an integer), `gain` is `g`, and `seconds` is `F / fs` (binary64, one division); every number is written as the shortest round-trip text of its binary64, as in every report. The `determinism_key` is the recipe's own (recipe hash, engine version, sample rate): it names the recipe, not the preview bytes, which the `preview` member marks. The render speed assertion counts the `F` frames rendered. A report made without `--preview` has no `preview` member and is unchanged byte for byte. The exit codes and stderr lines are unchanged apart from the usage error above.

**Preview set.** `tools/render_previews.py` renders the four starter layer fixtures (Spacing, Fixtures) and the two checklist fixtures below at 48 kHz into `build/previews/` (ignored by git), as `<fixture>.preview.wav` and `<fixture>.preview.report.json`. It refuses any output directory inside the repository that is not under `build/`. At -30 dB every one of the six has two layers in each ear, so each has `g = 0x1.030dc4ea03a72p-6`.

**Fixtures.** `preview-gamma-250-am-60-swell.json` is a 450 Hz carrier amplitude-modulated by the sine envelope at 6 Hz, 75 percent depth, and a 250 Hz sine carrier amplitude-modulated at 60 Hz with a smooth swell peaking at the 0 degree coupling phase, 100 percent depth, 180 s, no binaural layer and no loop. `preview-gamma-250-am-60-bursts.json` is the same with 50 ms bursts at 0 degrees. They put the 250 Hz carrier with coupled gamma AM at 60 Hz on the listening checklist (the carrier rule review of 2026-11-16, condition 2). Both pass the carrier rule (250 Hz is at least 4 times 60 Hz, 240 Hz) and the spacing rule over the whole session, worst pair the same in both ears: the swell, 128 Hz (margin 78 Hz) between the theta reference line `(0, -1, 0)` at 444 Hz and the gamma PAC line `(1, 1, 0)` at 316 Hz; the bursts, 92 Hz (margin 42 Hz) between that theta line and the gamma PAC burst line `(0, 17, 0)` at 352 Hz.

**Reference.** The Python reference (`reference/preview.py`, implemented from this section) computes `F`, the layer count per ear, and `g` by the steps above in binary64 (its square root is correctly rounded), so its `g` has the vector bits.

**Vectors.** The gain vectors give `10^(P / 20)` and `g` as bits with the exact `g` at 25 digits, for -30 dB with one, two, and three layers and for each branch of the steps (0, -10, -20, -40, -50, -430, and -440 dB). The layer vectors give the count per ear for the preview set, the Sleep layers with each ear muted, and one-layer, binaural-only, and no-layer fixtures. The frame vectors give `N`, `O`, `O + B`, `L`, `T`, `F`, and `F / fs` for the preview set at both rates.
