## Loudness

Story 4.1 writes integrated loudness and its measurement window. Loudness is a measurement, not an engine stage: two meters implement this section on their own, one in the Python assertion library (`tests/assertions/loudness.py`) and one in the command-line tooling (`cli/loudness.{hpp,cpp}`, in `arcani_cli_core`), and neither lives in `engine/`. Neither meter changes a sample, a vector, a fixture byte, a recipe hash, a pinned hash, or any render or report made without `--loudness`. Story 4.2 stores a loudness gain measured by this section; Story 4.3 adds true peak; Story 4.4 adds the ramps the window leaves out.

**Input.** A meter reads the binary64 render output (Stub chain, Output and taps), both channels, before 24-bit rounding: in measure mode every layer runs at unit amplitude and the master gain is 1.0, so a 24-bit WAV of a render with two layers clips and is not a measurement of the output. Channel 0 is left, channel 1 is right, and each has the BS.1770 channel weight `G = 1.0`. A meter is defined for any sample rate `fs` that is a positive multiple of 10 (so a block and a step are whole frames); the engine renders at 44.1 and 48 kHz (row `sample-rates`). These meters are not bit exact across platforms: the filter design calls `tan`, and the sums run in each meter's own order. That is why two meters exist and why they agree within row `loudness-meter-agreement`, not bit for bit.

**Rows.** `loudness-meter-calibration` (0.1 LU): each meter reads every synthesized Tech 3341 case within it. `loudness-meter-agreement` (0.1 LU): the two meters agree within it on every render of the agreement set. Both are the plus or minus 0.1 LU that EBU Tech 3341 v4.0 (November 2023), Table 1, states for every integrated loudness case. `loudness-window-min` (60 s): the shortest window, one period of range row `rate-cycle-period` (Audio, 2026-11-20). `loudness-tolerance` (1 LU): the loudness assertion. Design log 2026-11-20 (zz).

### K-weighting

ITU-R BS.1770-5 weights each channel by two second-order filters in series: a high shelf (stage 1, the acoustic effect of the head) and a high-pass (stage 2, the RLB curve). It prints their coefficients for 48 kHz only, to 14 decimal places, and requires the same response at any other rate. This section derives every rate's coefficients from one analog prototype per stage, found from the printed coefficients alone, so at 48 kHz the derivation gives the printed coefficients back.

**The printed coefficients** (BS.1770-5, Tables 1 and 2), with `a0 = 1`:

```
stage 1, shelf:      b0 =  1.53512485958697   b1 = -2.69169618940638   b2 = 1.19839281085285
                     a1 = -1.69065929318241   a2 =  0.73248077421585
stage 2, high-pass:  b0 =  1.0                b1 = -2.0                b2 = 1.0
                     a1 = -1.99004745483398   a2 =  0.99007225036621
```

**Prototype.** Each stage is the bilinear transform of a second-order analog section, prewarped at the stage's own frequency `f0`: with `K = tan(pi * f0 / fs)` and `D = 1 + K / Q + K^2`, the denominator is

```
a1 = 2 * (K^2 - 1) / D        a2 = (1 - K / Q + K^2) / D
```

and the numerator is, for the shelf, with `Vh` its high-frequency gain and `Vb` its band gain,

```
b0 = (Vh + Vb * K / Q + K^2) / D    b1 = 2 * (K^2 - Vh) / D    b2 = (Vh - Vb * K / Q + K^2) / D
```

and for the high-pass, with `G` its high-frequency gain,

```
b0 = G / D    b1 = -2 * G / D    b2 = G / D
```

Inverted at `fs = 48000` from the printed coefficients, in exact arithmetic (the generator uses 70-digit decimals):

```
K^2   = (1 + a1 + a2) / (1 - a1 + a2)        D = 4 / (1 - a1 + a2)
K / Q = (1 - a2) * D / 2                     f0 = 48000 * atan(K) / pi
shelf:      Vh = K^2 - b1 * D / 2            Vb = (b0 - b2) * D / (2 * K / Q)
high-pass:  G = b0 * D
```

The shelf's five printed coefficients have one more equation than unknowns, `b0 + b2 = 2 * (Vh + K^2) / D`, and it holds to the generator's precision; the printed high-pass numerator is exactly `G / D` times `(1, -2, 1)` with `G / D = 1`. So the prototype fits the printed filter exactly: shelf `f0` about 1681.974 Hz, `Q` about 0.70718, `Vh` about 1.58486 (+4.0 dB), `Vb` about 1.25872; high-pass `f0` about 38.135 Hz, `Q` about 0.50033, `G` about 1.00499 (Vectors: K-weighting prototype). The meters hold these seven values as the binary64 constants of that table.

**Coefficients at any rate.** In binary64, with the constants above and the C library `tan` (correctly rounded or not):

```
k  = tan(pi * f0 / fs)        pi the binary64 nearest pi; (pi * f0) / fs
kq = k / Q
d  = 1.0 + kq + k * k
a1 = 2.0 * (k * k - 1.0) / d
a2 = (1.0 - kq + k * k) / d
shelf:      b0 = (Vh + Vb * kq + k * k) / d    b1 = 2.0 * (k * k - Vh) / d    b2 = (Vh - Vb * kq + k * k) / d
high-pass:  b0 = G / d    b1 = -2.0 * G / d    b2 = G / d
```

Each binary64 coefficient is within `5e-15` of the ideal coefficient of Vectors: K-weighting coefficients (half a unit in the 14th decimal place, the precision BS.1770 prints), at both rates; at 48 kHz the ideal is the printed coefficient. This bound is the printed precision of the standard, not a tuned tolerance: a meter that misses it has the wrong filter.

**Filter.** Each channel runs stage 1, then stage 2, as the difference equation

```
y[n] = b0 * x[n] + b1 * x[n-1] + b2 * x[n-2] - a1 * y[n-1] - a2 * y[n-2]
```

from zero state (`x` and `y` before the first sample are 0) at the first sample of the measured signal. A meter may compute the same output another way (the Python meter convolves with the cascade's impulse response, truncated where its tail is below `1e-30` of its peak); the result is the same filter.

### Integrated loudness

ITU-R BS.1770-5, Annex 1, for a stereo signal of `T` frames:

```
B = 0.4 * fs                 block frames: a 400 ms gating block (17640 at 44.1 kHz, 19200 at 48 kHz)
S = B / 4                    step frames: blocks overlap by 75 percent, so one starts every 100 ms
J = floor(T / S) - 3         the block count: block j covers frames [j * S, j * S + B), j = 0 to J - 1
                             (no block when T < B)
z[j]  = (sum over both channels c of sum over block j of y_c[n]^2) / B
l[j]  = -0.691 + 10 * log10(z[j])                 block loudness, LUFS (-inf when z[j] = 0)
```

Since the weights are 1.0, `z[j]` is the sum of the two channels' mean squares. Gating, in this order:

```
absolute:  A = { j : l[j] > -70 }                                    Gamma_a = -70 LUFS
relative:  Gamma_r = -0.691 + 10 * log10(mean of z[j] over A) - 10   (-10 LU)
           R = { j in A : l[j] > Gamma_r }
integrated loudness  L = -0.691 + 10 * log10(mean of z[j] over R)    LUFS-I
```

Both comparisons are strict. When `A` or `R` is empty (no block, an all-silent signal, or every block below -70 LUFS), `L` is `-inf`: no exception and no NaN. A meter reports `L` as a binary64; `-inf` is written as JSON `null` in a report.

**Gating off.** For the must-fail meter only, `gating = false` takes `R` as every block: `L = -0.691 + 10 * log10(mean of z[j] over all J blocks)`, `-inf` when `J = 0` or that mean is 0. It must fail Tech 3341 cases 3 and 4 and read case 5 the same as with gating (Calibration).

**Steps in frames.** Because `S` divides `B`, a meter may sum `y^2` per step of `S` frames and form each block from four consecutive steps; frames past the last whole step belong to no block. The gating thresholds come from the block loudness `l[j]` exactly as written above.

### Calibration

Both meters are calibrated on EBU Tech 3341 v4.0 (November 2023), Table 1, cases 1 to 5, synthesized in the test harness from the levels and durations printed in that public document. The EBU's own test files are never downloaded or committed: the EBU terms of use (July 2019) forbid for-profit use and redistribution of its test sequences (Audio, 2026-11-20). Case 6 (5.1) and cases 7 and 8 (programme recordings) are out of scope.

**Signal.** Every case is a stereo 1 kHz sine, unmodulated, the level the per-channel peak, applied in phase to both channels. Segments play one after another with no gap; segment `s` at level `P_s` dBFS lasts `seconds_to_samples(duration_s, fs)` frames (Core), and for frame `n` of the whole signal (counted from 0 across all segments, so the phase runs on through every level change):

```
A_s  = 10^(P_s / 20)
x[n] = A_s * sin(2 * pi * ((1000 * n) mod fs) / fs)     the integer remainder is exact; both channels the same
```

**Pass.** Each meter reads each case at both rates within row `loudness-meter-calibration` of the Tech 3341 expected value (Vectors: Tech 3341 cases). The ideal columns of that table are the steady-state readings: every 100 ms step of a segment at its level's K-weighted power `A_s^2 * |H(1 kHz)|^2` (both channels), the gates as written, filter transients left out. Every segment boundary falls on a step boundary, and a step holds 100 whole cycles of the 1 kHz tone, so a meter's reading differs from the ideal only by the filter's response to each level change, far inside 0.1 LU. With gating off the ideal is case 3 about -24.16 LUFS and case 4 about -25.13 LUFS, both outside 0.1 LU of -23.0, and case 5 the same as gated.

### Window

The measurement window is the steady part of the session, every ramp and fade left out (AD-13; design log 2026-11-01 (w), (x)). Until Story 4.4 adds gain ramps, the steady part is the whole session or the body. With `N` the session end (Stub chain), `O` and `B` the opening end and the body (Loop, Frames), and `W_min = seconds_to_samples(loudness_window_min_s, fs)` (row `loudness-window-min`), every value an exact integer:

```
no loop:  window = frames [0, N), measured once                       accepted when N >= W_min
loop:     k = the smallest integer k >= 1 with k * B >= W_min         k = max(1, ceil(W_min / B))
          window = frames [O, O + B) of the render, measured k times in a row as one signal
```

So a non-looping recipe is measured over its whole session; Story 4.4 will narrow it to the longest stretch with no layer or master gain ramp active. A looping recipe is measured over its body only: one body pass, the body repeated a whole number of times to at least `W_min` frames when one pass is shorter (body 25 s: `k = 3`, 75 s). The repeated passes are the same samples, played again: frame `O + B - 1` of pass `i` is followed by frame `O` of pass `i + 1`, and the K-weighting filter state runs on across each repeat. Only the first window frame starts from zero filter state. Every looping body passes the window: the loop rules (Loop, Compile order) keep `B >= 1`.

**Window rule.** A non-looping recipe with `N < W_min` (a session shorter than 60 s) is rejected when loudness is measured: parameter `/length_s`, value `N / fs` (binary64, one division: the session length as rendered, written as the shortest round-trip text), rule `loudness_window_rule`, limit `>= 60 s (row loudness-window-min)`. It runs after compile and before any output is opened, so nothing is written. It runs only when loudness is measured: `arcani-render --loudness` and `arcani-render --store-gain` (Loudness gain, Store). A plain render, in either mode, of a recipe shorter than 60 s is unchanged.

**Whole render.** Every `--loudness` report also gives the integrated loudness of the whole render, frames `[0, N)` from zero filter state: the reading the Epic 4 whole-render ceiling (never more than 1 LU above target) will judge. For a non-looping recipe it is the window reading.

### Assertion

`check_loudness(x, fs, target)` (Python) is the loudness assertion against row `loudness-tolerance`: `measured = L - target` in LU, pass when `|measured| <= 1` LU (inclusive). A signal with any NaN or infinite sample fails with note `not finite` and `measured` null; a signal whose `L` is `-inf` fails with note `silent` and `measured` null. A render scaled in the harness to read the target passes; the same render scaled to read 1.5 LU above the target fails. It has the report shape of every assertion (Story 1.4): name `loudness`, row, threshold, unit, measured, result, note.

**Report assertions.** In final mode, `--loudness` adds the assertions `loudness` and `loudness_whole` (Loudness gain, Final-mode checks). A measure-mode render runs at unit gain and reads far above -36 LUFS-I, so its report carries the readings, never a pass or fail for loudness.

### Report

`arcani-render RECIPE --out WAV --loudness` (no value) adds a member `loudness` after `preview` (or `spacing`, `loop`, `frames`, whichever comes last) and before `determinism_key`; for example (the readings here are only an illustration):

```
  "loudness": {
    "window_start_frame": 2880000,
    "window_body_frames": 5760000,
    "window_repeats": 1,
    "window_frames": 5760000,
    "window_lufs_i": -3.5,
    "whole_lufs_i": -3.5
  },
```

`window_start_frame` is `O` (0 without a loop), `window_body_frames` is `B` (`N` without a loop), `window_repeats` is `k` (1 without a loop), `window_frames` is `k * B` (the frames measured), `window_lufs_i` and `whole_lufs_i` are the two readings as the shortest round-trip text of their binary64, `null` for `-inf`. The WAV is the render as without `--loudness`, byte for byte, and so is every other report member. `--loudness` may be given once; twice, or with `--preview`, is a usage error (exit 2, nothing written). It works in both compile modes. A report made without `--loudness` has no `loudness` member and is unchanged byte for byte. The render speed assertion counts only the main render, not the repeated window passes.

**Agreement set.** Every fixture in `recipes/fixtures/valid/` that compiles in measure mode at both rates and whose window is accepted (Vectors: loudness windows, result `accepted`): both meters measure its window at both rates, the Python meter on the tap dump output (`arcani-tapdump`, the same engine output), the command-line meter in the report, and they agree within row `loudness-meter-agreement`. For the whole-render fixtures of `loudness_constants.whole_fixtures` the whole render is compared too. An all-silent window reads `-inf` (`null`) in both.

## Loudness gain

Story 4.2 writes the stored loudness gain (AD-13). The command-line renderer measures a recipe once, in measure mode, over the window of Loudness, and writes two members into the recipe file: `loudness_gain_db` (the gain) and `loudness_hash` (the loudness hash of the content it was measured for). Final mode checks both at compile and applies the gain in the master chain. The meter, the gain rounding, and the file rewrite are tooling outside `engine/`; the engine computes the loudness hash, checks the stored members, and multiplies by the gain. In measure mode the gain is 1.0, so no measure-mode render, tap, report, recipe hash, or pinned hash changes.

**Rows.** Range row `loudness-gain`: -60 dB to 0 dB, inclusive (Audio, 2026-11-25; design log 2026-11-25 (aaa)). The stored gain can only turn the sound down, and a recipe that would need a boost is refused. It is an engineering safety bound, not a research value. Rows `loudness-window-min` (60 s) and `loudness-tolerance` (1 LU) are used as in Loudness. The gain rounding step (0.01 dB) and the constant `c` are design log 2026-11-25 (bbb).

### Loudness hash

The loudness hash names the recipe content a stored gain was measured for. Let `M` be the top-level members whose schema tag (`x-arcani-tag`, recipes/README.md, Tags) is `loudness-relevant`: in v0.1 `length_s`, `loudness_target_lufs`, `theta_reference`, `gamma_pac`, `binaural`, and `loop`. The hash covers the object that holds exactly the members of `M` the recipe has, each with its whole value (every member inside a layer object or `loop` is tagged `loudness-relevant` too):

```
O_L = { m: recipe[m]  for each m in M that the recipe has }
H   = lowercase hex of SHA-256(RFC 8785 canonical UTF-8 bytes of O_L)
```

The strict parse, the canonical form, and the hex are the recipe hash's own (recipes/README.md, Recipe hash); only the member set differs. `schema_version`, `engine_version`, and `seed` (tag `none`) are left out, and so is every `hash-excluded` member (`author`, `date`, `rationale`, `loudness_gain_db`, `loudness_hash`). So editing the metadata, the seed, the version fields, the stored gain, or the stored hash, or reformatting the file (whitespace, member order, or a number written another way that parses to the same binary64), keeps `H`; changing any loudness-relevant value, or adding or removing a layer or the loop, changes it. v0.1 has no assets. An engine change that moves the level does not change `H`; the final-mode loudness checks on every measured fixture in CI catch it instead (Final-mode checks).

The engine computes `H` at compile, in both modes, from the parsed recipe and the tags of its field table, and `arc_plan_loudness_hash` returns it. `tools/recipe_hash.py` computes it independently, for the Python cross-checks. Vectors: loudness hash gives `O_L` in canonical form, `H`, and the recipe hash for every valid fixture that carries a `loudness_hash` and for both store fixtures.

### Gain rounding

From the window reading `L` (LUFS-I, binary64: the command-line meter over the window, Loudness, Window) and the recipe's `loudness_target_lufs` `T`, each step one correctly rounded binary64 operation unless marked exact:

```
d = T - L                 the needed gain in dB, one rounding
x = 100.0 * d             one rounding
k = round(x)              exact: the integer nearest x, a tie away from zero (C++ std::round)
v = k / 100.0             one correctly rounded division: the stored gain in dB
```

Then the range check, first failure wins, each a rejection with parameter `/loudness_gain_db`:

- `L = -inf` (a silent window: no block passed the gates) gives `d = x = +inf`: value `inf`, rule `range.max`, limit `<= 0 dB (row loudness-gain)`. The meter reads only finite samples, so `L` is finite or `-inf`, and `x` is finite in every other case.
- `v < -60`: value the shortest round-trip text of `v`, rule `range.min`, limit `>= -60 dB (row loudness-gain)`.
- `v > 0`: value the shortest round-trip text of `v`, rule `range.max`, limit `<= 0 dB (row loudness-gain)`.

These are the value, rule, and limit the range check at compile gives for a stored `v` (Final compile), so the store refuses exactly the gains that compile would refuse. The bounds are the row's values, read from `tests/tables/ranges.tsv` by the command-line tooling before anything is opened.

**Why round.** On one build the meter gives the same reading every time. Across platforms and meter orders it may differ in its last bits, since the meters call `tan` and `log10` (Loudness, Input). The rounding to 0.01 dB leaves `k` unchanged by such a difference unless `x` lies within a few units in the last place of a half step, so measuring the same recipe twice writes the same bytes. It moves the gain by at most 0.005 dB, plus the roundings of `d` and `x` (below 1e-12 dB), against the 1 LU tolerance. Only the dev PC writes gains (Store).

**Gain text.** `k` is written as a decimal with exactly two decimals: `-` when `k < 0`, then `|k| div 100` in decimal digits with no leading zero (zero written `0`), then `.`, then `|k| mod 100` as two digits. So `k = -3527` is `-35.27`, `-6000` is `-60.00`, `-5` is `-0.05`, and `0` is `0.00`, never `-0.00`. The text parses to exactly `v`: both are the binary64 nearest `k / 100` (the recipe parser is correctly rounded, and `k / 100.0` is one correctly rounded division of two exact operands).

### Linear gain

At compile, the recipe's `loudness_gain_db` value `v` (binary64, as parsed; the range check has held it to -60 to 0 dB) gives the step count and the linear gain:

```
x = 100.0 * v             one rounding
k = round(x)              exact: the integer nearest x, a tie away from zero; -6000 <= k <= 0

c = 0x1.004b7e9b5ce5dp+0  the binary64 nearest 10^(1/2000) = 1.001151955538168876984203...
m = |k|,  p = 1.0,  b = c
while m > 0:
    if m is odd:  p = p * b           one rounding
    m = m div 2                       exact
    if m > 0:     b = b * b           one rounding
g = p                     when k >= 0
g = 1.0 / p               when k < 0, one rounding
```

For every gain text the store writes, `k` is the stored integer and `g` is close to `10^(k / 2000) = 10^(v / 20)`. A value written by hand between two steps applies the nearest step, a tie away from zero: `-35.275` gives `x = -3527.5` and `k = -3528`. The steps use only multiplication, division, and exact integer operations (AD-9), so `g` has the same bits on every platform, in the engine and in the Python reference, which both follow them in this order. At `k = 0`, `g = 1.0` exactly. Over every storable `k` (0 to -6000), `g` is within a relative 3.76e-13 of `10^(k / 2000)` (largest at `k = -5999`), about 3.3e-12 dB: almost all of it is the rounding of `c` itself, raised to the power `|k|`. The generator checks `c` against both binary64 neighbors in 70-digit decimal arithmetic and computes that largest error over all 6001 values of `k`.

### Applied gain

The master chain's per-channel gain (Stub chain, Per sample):

```
measure mode:  g_L = g_R = 1.0
final mode:    g_L = g_R = g              Linear gain, from the recipe's loudness_gain_db
master[c][n] = join[c][n] * g_c           one rounding
```

The plan holds the gain (`master_gain`), so the render path has no mode branch. Since `y * 1.0 = y` for every binary64 `y`, every measure-mode render, tap, and report is unchanged bit for bit, and so is a final render whose stored gain is `0.00`. The gain scales only the master tap and the output: the theta, gamma, bus, and join taps are the same in both modes. Both channels get the same `g`, and no stage reads one channel to write the other, so a muted ear's channel at the master tap is its stereo bus times `g`, and binaural isolation is unchanged. The Python reference (`reference/stub_chain.py`) takes the mode and the stored gain from the recipe and applies `g` by the same steps; a final render matches it below -100 dBFS.

### Final compile

The range check on `loudness_gain_db` (row `loudness-gain`) runs in both modes, with the other range checks, in field table order right after `loudness_target_lufs`, whenever the member is present: rule `range.min` with limit `>= -60 dB (row loudness-gain)`, or rule `range.max` with limit `<= 0 dB (row loudness-gain)`, value the shortest round-trip text of the parsed value. In final mode, after the spacing rule, in this order, first failure wins:

1. `loudness_gain_db` absent: parameter `/loudness_gain_db`, value `absent`, rule `final.required`, limit `present in final mode` (unchanged).
2. `loudness_hash` absent: parameter `/loudness_hash`, the same (unchanged).
3. The stored `loudness_hash` is not `H`: parameter `/loudness_hash`, value the stored hash, rule `final.loudness_hash`, limit `= ` followed by the 64 hex digits of `H` computed for this recipe. The comparison is exact on the 64 lowercase hex digits (the schema pattern allows no other form).

Measure mode never compares the stored hash. So a final compile accepts a recipe whose metadata (`author`, `date`, `rationale`), seed, or version fields were edited after storing, and rejects one whose loudness-relevant content changed.

### Store

`arcani-render RECIPE --store-gain [--rate 44100|48000]` (no value; the rate defaults to 48000). With `--out`, `--report`, `--preview`, `--mode`, or `--loudness`, or with `--store-gain` given twice, it is a usage error: exit 2, nothing read or written. The steps run in this order, and nothing is written before step 8:

1. Read rows `loudness-window-min` and `loudness-gain`. A table error exits 3.
2. Read the bytes of RECIPE and compile them in measure mode at the rate. A rejection prints the usual `rejected:` line and exits 1.
3. The window rule (Loudness, Window). A rejection prints its `rejected:` line and exits 1.
4. Render the window only, into the command-line meter: a looping recipe's frames `[O, O + B)` once for each pass of the window (its repeat count, Loudness, Window), each pass by `arc_seek` to `O`; a non-looping recipe's frames `[0, N)`. No other frame is rendered. `arc_seek` is pure and the meter's reading does not depend on how the frames are split, so `L` equals, bit for bit, the `window_lufs_i` of a measure-mode `--loudness` render at the same rate.
5. The gain rounding with `T` the recipe's `loudness_target_lufs` (read by the member scanner of step 6 from the accepted bytes and converted by `std::from_chars`, which is correctly rounded, so it is the binary64 the engine parsed). A range rejection prints `rejected: parameter=/loudness_gain_db value=<value> rule=<rule> limit=<limit>` and exits 1.
6. The file rewrite (File rewrite) of the bytes with the gain text of `k` and `H` from the measure-mode plan (`arc_plan_loudness_hash`).
7. Compile the new bytes in final mode at the same rate. By construction they are accepted (the same content, a stored gain in the row, and the matching hash). A rejection here is an internal fault: it prints the `rejected:` line and exits 3, and nothing is written.
8. When the new bytes equal the old ones, nothing is written. Otherwise they are written to `RECIPE.partial` in the same directory, which is then renamed over RECIPE. On any failure the partial file is removed, RECIPE is unchanged, and the exit is 3.
9. Print one line to stdout, `stored: loudness_gain_db=<gain text> loudness_hash=<H> window_lufs_i=<L>` (`L` as its shortest round-trip text), and exit 0.

Storing a second time reads the same `L` (step 4: the measure compile ignores the stored members), so it gives the same `k`, the same `H`, and the same bytes. The store writes no WAV and no report, and the session outside the window is never rendered. Only the dev PC stores gains; no CI job writes one, and a CI check fails any run that leaves a file under `recipes/` changed or added (Story 4.2).

**Store fixtures.** The tests that store a gain work on temp copies of `starter-focus-layers.json` (the Story 4.1 assertion fixture: looping, 45 min, a 120 s body measured once) and `theta-cycle-sine.json` (non-looping, 120 s, measured whole), never on the checkout.

### File rewrite

The rewrite runs only on bytes the engine has accepted (strict JSON, no duplicate member names, a schema-valid recipe), so each top-level member of the root object is well defined. The member scanner reads, for each one in file order: its lead (the whitespace between the preceding `{` or `,` and the key's opening quote), its key token, its separator (every byte from just after the key's closing quote to the value's first byte: whitespace, `:`, whitespace), and its value token. Names are compared after JSON unescaping, so `"loudness_hash"` is `loudness_hash`. The new value texts are the gain text for `loudness_gain_db` and `"` + `H` + `"` for `loudness_hash`.

- **Present member:** its value token is replaced by the new value text. Its lead, key token, and separator are kept.
- **Missing members:** inserted as one run right after the value token of `loudness_target_lufs` (schema-required, so always present), in the order `loudness_gain_db`, then `loudness_hash`, whichever are missing. Each is `,` + the lead of `loudness_target_lufs` + the member name as a JSON string + the separator of `loudness_target_lufs` + the new value text.
- **Every other byte** is kept: whitespace, line endings, member order, number spellings, escapes, and anything after the root object.

So the rewrite never changes a loudness-relevant byte or any member the recipe hash covers: the new bytes have the same `H` and the same recipe hash. Applied to its own output with the same gain text and hash, it gives the same bytes. The command-line tooling (`cli/recipe_write.{hpp,cpp}`) and the vector generator implement it on their own. Vectors: file rewrite gives the input and output of each case as JSON strings; each output compiles in final mode.

### Final-mode checks

`arcani-render RECIPE --out WAV --mode final --loudness` adds two assertions after the render speed assertion, in its shape (Story 1.4: name, row, threshold, unit, measured, result, note), both on row `loudness-tolerance` (1 LU), with `T` the recipe's `loudness_target_lufs` (read as in Store, step 5):

- `loudness`: measured `window_lufs_i - T` in LU (one binary64 subtraction), pass when `|measured| <= 1` (inclusive). The steady part reads the target.
- `loudness_whole`: measured `whole_lufs_i - T` in LU, pass when `measured <= 1` (inclusive). The whole-render ceiling: ramps and fades are quieter by design, so it has no lower bound.

A reading of `-inf` (silent) gives measured `null`, result `fail`, note `silent`; otherwise the note is empty. A failed assertion exits 4 with the WAV and the report written, as for every assertion. A measure-mode `--loudness` report has neither assertion (a measure render runs at unit gain and reads far above the target), and a final-mode render without `--loudness` has neither; both reports are unchanged byte for byte.

**Must-fail.** The harness adds 1.5 dB to the stored gain of a stored temp copy (`k + 150`, the gain text rewritten; the gain is hash-excluded, so the copy still compiles in final mode). Its final render reads about 1.5 LU above `T` and fails `loudness` (and `loudness_whole`), exit 4.

**Final fixture.** `stub-chain-1s-beat-6.json` keeps its placeholder gain `-1.5` (`k = -150`) with its real loudness hash: a 1 s final-mode render for the Python reference match and the four-platform hash check at 44.1 and 48 kHz. It is shorter than 60 s, so it is never measured.

**Vectors.** The loudness hash vectors give `O_L` in canonical form, `H`, and the recipe hash per fixture: the metadata, seed, engine version, schema minor, and reformatted variants of `both-layers.json` share its `H` and not its recipe hash, and the beat rate, carrier, theta carrier, length, and loudness target variants differ from it. The gain rounding vectors give `d`, `x`, `k`, and the gain text or the rejection for chosen readings at `T = -36`: ties away from zero, readings that round to `0.00` from either side, both row bounds, a needed boost, and a silent window. The stored gain vectors give `v`, `x`, `k`, and `g` (hex and bits) with the exact `10^(k / 2000)` at 25 digits for two-decimal texts from `0.00` to `-60.00` and for hand-written values between steps. The file rewrite vectors give each case's input and output for gain text `-35.27`.

Per stored gain text: the parsed value `v`, `x = 100.0 * v`, `k`, whether the text is the two-decimal gain text of `k`, the linear gain `g` (hex and bits), and the exact `10^(k / 2000)` at 25 digits (Loudness gain, Linear gain).

## 24-bit rounding

The command-line renderer (Story 1.4) writes the render output (Stub chain, Output and taps) as 24-bit PCM. Each binary64 output sample `s` becomes one signed 24-bit integer `v` by this rule. There is no dither and no other shaping.

```
if s is NaN:            v = 0                      any sign or payload
x = s * 0x1p23          binary64, exact (see below)
if x >= 8388607:        v = 8388607                +1.0, everything above full scale, +inf
else if x <= -8388608:  v = -8388608               -1.0, everything below, -inf
else:                   v = x rounded to the nearest integer, ties to even
```

`v` is written as 3 bytes, little-endian two's complement: the bytes of `v mod 2^24`, least significant first. Samples are written in render order, left then right per frame.

**Exactness.** Scaling by 2^23 is exact for every finite `s`: it only changes the exponent, a subnormal `s` moves toward the normal range and loses no bits, and a product too large for binary64 becomes an infinity of the same sign, which the clamp treats the same as the exact value. The comparisons are exact. In the last branch `|x| < 2^23`, so `x` and its fractional part `x - floor(x)` are exact binary64 values, and the tie test compares that part with exactly 1/2. No step depends on the host rounding mode or denormal mode: a subnormal `s` gives `v = 0` whether or not the host flushes it.

**Range.** The 24-bit range is asymmetric: +1.0 maps to 8388607 (2^23 - 1), and -1.0 to -8388608 (-2^23). Signed zeros and every `|x| < 1/2` give 0; `|x|` exactly 1/2 also gives 0 (ties to even).

### Vectors: 24-bit rounding

`s` and `x` are hex floats (`nan`, `inf`, and `-inf` as written). Rules: `clamp` (at or past full scale), `nan`, `tie` (`x` exactly k + 1/2), `round` (nearest, not a tie), and `zero` (signed zeros and values that round to 0 without a tie). Bytes are the 3 written bytes in file order, as hex.

| Rule | Case | s (hex) | x = s * 2^23 (hex) | Value | Bytes (little-endian) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
